Project Native — Design Document v1

An operating system whose substrate is workflows of agents — so the person running their company can focus on the work that matters.

§ES Executive Summary #

Read this first. Two pages. No prior context required. The rest of the document is the receipt for every claim made here.

What it is, in one line

Native gives the person running a company an AI chief of staff — a system that takes work off your plate, makes the calls you'd make, asks when it shouldn't guess, and remembers everything so it gets sharper every week.

Why now

The person running a company spends 60-80% of their week on context-switching. The same decisions get re-decided. The same context gets re-explained to every tool, every meeting, every assistant. Existing tools are chatbots (answer one question, then forget) or trackers (record work but don't do it). Nothing actually compounds across your real work.

How it works — the stack

                    YOU (running your company)
                                │
                                ▼
              ┌───────────────────────────────────┐
              │  CHIEF OF STAFF  (the product)    │
              │  — what you talk to                │
              └────────────────┬──────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
              ┌───────────────────────────────────┐
              │  Workflow of agents                │
              │  (specialists, generalists,        │
              │   project managers — the team)     │
              └────────────────┬──────────────────┘
                               │
                               ▼
              ┌───────────────────────────────────┐
              │  Slack · email · tools             │
              │  (how the team reaches the         │
              │  world on your behalf)             │
              └───────────────────────────────────┘

The bottom layer is a workforce of AI agents — some specialists, some generalists, some project managers. The product on top (Chief of Staff today; Project Manager and others later) is the surface you actually talk to. Native picks the right product, and the right team inside it, for the task you bring.

What a typical turn looks like

   You ask          The system        Agents do          You see        And it
   in your   ───▶   thinks about ───▶ the work    ───▶   what was ───▶  remembers
   own words        it; asks you      (or pause                 done            │
                    if it's unsure    to ask you                                │
                    about anything    on the calls                              │
                    consequential)    only you                                  │
                          ▲           should make)                              │
                          │                                                     │
                          └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                next time you ask something similar,
                                it starts further along

You bring an ask — in your own words, not in some form. The system figures out what you mean, gathers what it already knows about your work, drafts the answer or runs the task, and either does it (if it's safe and routine) or asks you (if it's a real call only you should make). Every step is recorded; next week, when you ask something similar, the system starts further along.

What's inside, in plain words

PieceWhat it doesWhy you care
CharterA short, written agreement: "for this part of my work, here's what you can do on your own, and here's where you must ask me."You stay in control. Nothing happens that you didn't authorize, at some level.
Authority gateBefore any outbound action (sending an email, writing a doc, paying a bill), the system checks the Charter. Default-go if you've said it's safe; ask otherwise.No surprises. The system never sends something in your name without your permission.
Audit logEvery decision the system makes is recorded as a row you can replay end-to-end.If something looks wrong, you can rewind and see exactly what happened.
Test before deliveryThe system checks its own output against what you asked for, before showing it to you.You don't get sloppy AI output. ≥80% first-time-right is the v1 target.
Boundary between scopesStrict wall between separate scopes of work (different companies, different projects). The system never crosses without you saying so.Sensitive info from one scope never leaks into another's work.
Voice + memoryThe system learns how you write, decide, and frame trade-offs.By week 8, the output sounds like you — because it learns your patterns, not generic AI patterns.

What "working" looks like in v1

  • Your cognitive load drops 40%+ on routine work within 8 weeks.
  • The system gets ≥80% of routine outputs right the first time.
  • The output sounds like you by week 8 — your voice, your decisions visible.
  • Anything it did in the last day can be replayed in 30 seconds.

The product roadmap

  • Today: Chief of Staff — the first product on Native. For the person running their company.
  • Next: Project Manager — for running multi-week initiatives end-to-end.
  • Then: more products as we learn what shapes operators need. The substrate (the agent workforce + the rules) stays the same; the products change.

Where to read next

If you have…Open these
5 more minutes§0 (a Tuesday today vs. a Tuesday eight weeks in) and §1.1 (how the layers fit)
20 minutes+ §2.B (the five things Chief of Staff does for you) + §4 (how the agent workforce is scoped + supervised)
60 minutes+ §3 (the methodology — how Native picks the right approach for each task) + §7 (how we know it's working)
Going deep+ §10 (research foundations · 105 source findings) + the architecture decision records (ADRs) under §9

One thing to remember: the document body specifies what you, the operator, observe. The engine-level architecture — type signatures, state machines, fail-safes — lives in canon files (linked throughout). Every claim in this PRD has a pointer to its source; nothing is hand-waved.

§0 What we are bringing into the world #

For: a person reading Native for the first time, in their first 90 seconds with this document.

What we want you to feel by the end: someone has thought hard about your work, your expertise can finally compound, and you are not alone in the fight.

A Tuesday, today

It is Tuesday, 9am. Paisa's Slack has a debate over loan-policy thresholds for the MVP cohort. DayFlow's reviewer kicked the APK back over a permissions string. Dharmik's board update is due Thursday, and you still owe Testlify a product-review pass before the founder check-in.

By 11am you have answered the same three questions you answered last Tuesday — what is the right risk threshold for Paisa, who owns the Play Store fix at DayFlow, what is the one slide that decides Dharmik's board. None of those decisions write back anywhere. Wednesday you will re-explain the context to whichever assistant or person asks.

The week is full. The substrate is empty. The bleed underneath the calendar is not the meetings — it is the work that never compounds.

A Tuesday, eight weeks in

Same Tuesday, 9am. CoS — running on Native — has already pulled the three Charters that frame today's work: Paisa risk-policy, DayFlow Play Store fix, Dharmik board prep.

The Paisa loan-policy debate arrived in Slack at 7am. CoS drafted three positions with the prior decision-provenance attached, surfaced the one threshold that needs your judgment, and queued the rest to auto-execute on your approval.

The DayFlow APK fix was picked up by an agent under your Charter overnight. The reviewer's reply is drafted in your voice; the patch is in a PR awaiting your authorize.

The Dharmik board slide is drafted from last quarter's pattern plus this quarter's metrics. One paragraph needs your eye.

Three decisions today, not nine. Every one of them writes back. Next Tuesday starts further ahead.

The two names you will hear

NameWhat it is
NativeThe platform underneath. Deploys, scopes, and supervises a workforce of agents on your behalf. You do not see it during use.
Chief of Staff (CoS)The first product on Native. An implementation consultant for the operator (person in the company). Frames the work, executes where the path is known, surfaces decisions where your judgment is needed, writes back what was decided.

Both terms get full spec later. §2 for CoS. §4 for Native. For now you only need to know they exist.

The problem we name

Operators spend 60-80% of the week on context-switching.

The same decision gets re-decided. The same context gets re-explained.

Existing tools are conversational or chore-based. Nothing compounds across your actual work.

Your expertise stays trapped in your head. Your day's output stays bounded by your day's hours.

Why this isn't another assistant

Existing toolsProject Native
Chatbots — answer one question at a timeWorkflow-shaped — runs a whole task end-to-end · loops feedback into substrate
Calendar / task managers — track workImplementation consultant — DOES the work; takes idea → operationalized state
LLM assistants — stateless, single-tenantCompounding substrate · multi-Charter · per-company tenancy · audit-replayable
RPA / agentic frameworks — fully autonomousOperator-in-loop on material decisions · trust earned via Authority Model · learned voice

The founder, in their own words

"I'm building the system I needed at Asawa. One that operationalizes how I think across companies — not just one that drafts emails."
— Sankalp Asawa, founder

The first operator, in their own words

"I stopped re-explaining context to my tools after week 4. By week 8, CoS was making the calls I would have made — in my voice, with my reasoning visible — and surfacing only the calls that genuinely needed me."
— ILLUSTRATIVE PLACEHOLDER · to be replaced with a real pilot-operator quote after the first cohort reaches week-8 expansion state

What "working" looks like in v1

  • Operator's cognitive load drops 40%+ on routine work within 8 weeks of onboarding.
  • CoS accuracy ≥80% first-time-test pass across task tiers T0-T3 (see §4.5 Test-before-conclude + §7.1.A Accuracy KPI).
  • Operator's working voice + decision patterns visible in every output by week 8 (engagement state S4 stabilized, see §2.E).
  • Full audit-replay of any past turn within 30s for a single-day window (see §2.B B5.c).

How you get there

Eight to twelve weeks. Five stages. Discovery (week 0) → Articulation (week 1) → Pilot (weeks 2-3) → Expansion (weeks 4-8) → Steady state (week 9+). Full journey at §6.5 Transformation Cycle.

What's next in this document

Everything above is the promise. Everything below is the receipt.

§1 Architecture shows the operating model that keeps the promise. §3 HOW Methodology is the doctrine the model runs on. §2 Chief of Staff is the product surface the operator actually meets.

§1 Architecture — multi-layer system #

This section is the canonical operating model. Every later section (§2-§6) instantiates §1; nothing re-explains the system. If a fact about the system belongs anywhere, it belongs here first.

§1.0 System architecture — the runtime at a glance

The system as it actually runs. The operator asks through the UI; the request runs on the Host (Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one); inside the always-on Daemon, Orchestration translates the request and finds the relevant process to run; the System of Process runs it (this is the runtime), gathering context from the System of Record + the External World and writing output back to the System of Record. Authority + Tenancy gates every access; Host + daemon run on the Compute (host machine). Second-level detail (context-build · verify · connectors) lives inside System of Process — see §4.

§1.1 below gives the complementary view — the same system cut by layer ownership (who owns what).

§1.0.1 Second-order block detail — design drafts (2026-06-11)

Standalone sources: _block-*.html · _matrix.html · _consumer-product.html.

Jump: UI (Consumer Product) · Pillar × Block matrix · Orchestration · System of Process · System of Record · Authority + Tenancy · Host (Claude CLI) · Compute · External World

WORKING DRAFT 2026-06-11 — not locked; founder review pending · re-cut per founder-accepted DeepSeek review.

First layer: Human → UI (here) → Host → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process} → SoR / External World · back to first-layer schema (sec.1.0)

Internal parts — the MECE re-cut (with what was absorbed where)

PartWhatFolded from (absorbed here)Doc home
Channels Transport in/out the operator already uses. Inbound Day-1 modalities: text chat, Slack mention, email forward, scheduled prompt; Future: voice, screenshot (B.1). Outbound channel picked by 3-rule order — explicit channel stated > standing preference > default-to-origin — with one-step escalation override when time-sensitive AND default channel is async (D.3). — section-2-f-2-input-intent
Ask Utterance → intent read → journey fan-out. Every utterance resolves to one of 5 intent types (Task / Query / Directive / Feedback / New Idea, B.2) and fans out to journeys C.1–C.5 (B.5). Multi-turn carry: recent intent, named entities, active charter, deferred follow-ups, open questions (B.3). Trust moments now live INSIDE this flow: confirm-back below confidence threshold (B.4 high/medium/low/very-low ladder) and the ask-before-acting gate (F.1/F.5: irreversible → multi-step confirm; high-risk → one-tap confirm; new authority → grant once/always/no; first-time channel → preview before send). Trust moments (ask-side: B.4 confirm-back below threshold + F.1/F.5 ask-before-acting boundary, grounded at section-2-f-6-trust-authority) section-2-f-2-input-intent
Receive How Native talks back. 6 response modalities (M1 text reply, M2 Slack ping, M3 email, M4 summary card, M5 approval-ask card, M6 scheduled reminder — D.1), 3 landing times (sync / scheduled / async — D.2), modality routing by ask shape (D.4). Standing surfaces are now a CASE OF Receive: Daily Pulse (H.3, four fixed blocks Yesterday/Open/Suggested/Signal), capability map (H.1 by-job/by-domain/by-stage), proactive capability surfacing (H.2), weekly retro (H.4) — all scheduled/proactive Receive deliveries. Receive-side trust moments fold in as the M5 approval-ask card, reversibility tags (F.2 reversible / reversible-with-effort / not-reversible), and on-demand audit replay (F.3 “show me what you did and why”). Standing surfaces (Daily Pulse + “what can you do” capability map + weekly retro, grounded at section-2-f-8-capability-pulse — now a case of scheduled/proactive Receive); Trust moments (receive-side: M5 approval-ask card + F.2 reversibility signals + F.3 audit visibility, grounded at section-2-f-6-trust-authority) section-2-f-4-outputs
Need-surfacesRENDER-ONLY RENDER-ONLY annotation, not an engine part. Maps each ask to the operator's recurring durable demands — the 5 jobs-to-be-done (JTBD-1 Task, JTBD-2 Query, JTBD-3 Directive, JTBD-4 Feedback, JTBD-5 New Idea, A.2) across the 4 personas (A.1). Describes how surfaces are discovered/justified (“which need does this ask serve”); must NOT leak into the engine as a part. — section-2-f-1-foundations
AdaptationRENDER-ONLY RENDER-ONLY annotation, not an engine part. Skill modes per A.9 (SM1 idea-only high-touch/conservative authority, SM2 idea+output medium-touch/D.6 default thresholds, SM3 idea+output+how low-touch/aggressive — detected from utterance, prior patterns, Charter scope) plus the §2.F.E personalization layer (per-domain skill-mode map E.1, charter & declared preferences E.2, layered memory E.3, forgetting E.4). Describes how Ask/Receive surfaces adapt per operator and per domain; must NOT leak into the engine as a part. — a9-skill-modes

Operator-visible states — the states strip

StateWhat the operator seesDoc home
error Operator-visible failure surfaces: misclassified-intent confirm-back with yes/no/clarify (G.1, one focused question max); failed action stated plainly in operator words with 3 recovery options, no stack traces or codes (G.2 retry-now/retry-later/cancel, wait-and-retry/make-copy/cancel, retry-misses/show-which/leave-as-is); operator overrides “stop” / “undo” / “different approach” (G.3); wrong-answer retry loop where two consecutive misses escalate to operator walkthrough (G.4); every correction visibly acknowledged and carried forward, never silent (G.5). section-2-f-7-errors-recovery
empty The “I don't know what to ask for” surface. On-demand: structured capability map organized by job (5 JTBDs), by domain, and by stage — live today / available-unused / unlocks-on-grant (H.1). Before the operator opens the surface: Daily Pulse (H.3). Proactive surfacing on adjacent-ask, repeated-manual (3x), and dormant-fit triggers — soft offers that never block and decay after two declines (H.2). Re-engagement when dormant: “no new asks in 5 days” surfaces a dormant capability framed against current pain (I.6). section-2-f-8-capability-pulse
onboarding First session (~10 min): light charter setup + quick persona read (P1–P4) + a first REAL job, not a tutorial — by minute 10 one piece of actual work is done and the loop is felt once (I.1). Then staged: Day 1 (first job + quick follow-ups, directives queued, I.2), Week 1 (charter ratifies, first 2–3 standing directives fire, first Daily Pulse lands, I.3), Month 1 (mature ask-act-report-learn loop, OHS 3–6 band, I.4), Day 90+ (habit-formed, I.5), with drop-off triggers + recovery moves (I.6: pulse unopened 3 days → shrink to 3 lines; routine turned off → confirm, log why, offer leaner version). section-2-f-9-lifecycle
notification Outbound landings the operator did not just ask for: scheduled deliveries at a stated moment (08:00 digest, “ping me Friday”, M6 scheduled reminder) and async completion reports with receipt confirmed up front (D.2); Slack ping M2 when a standing routine completed or operator asked for Slack delivery; escalation override promotes the channel one step (chat → Slack DM → SMS-tier) and notes why, only when time-sensitive AND default channel is async (D.3). Output discipline: default to least-noise (D intro). Cadence/quiet-hours bounds come from the charter (E.2). section-2-f-4-outputs

Contracts with neighbor blocks

NeighborDirectionPayloadSource
Human in (Human → UI) Operator utterance — freeform intent resolving to one of 5 intent types (Task / Query / Directive / Feedback / New Idea), arriving over Day-1 input modalities (text chat, Slack mention, email forward, scheduled prompt; voice + screenshot marked Future), with multi-turn context carried across turns (recent intent, named entities, active charter, deferred follow-ups, open questions). section-2-f-2-input-intent
Human out (UI → Human) Delivery in one of 6 modalities (M1 text reply, M2 Slack ping, M3 email, M4 summary card, M5 approval-ask card, M6 scheduled reminder) at one of 3 landing times (sync / scheduled / async), channel selected by 3-rule order (explicit > standing preference > origin) with time-sensitive escalation override; carries the operator-visible states (error, empty/capability, onboarding, notification). section-2-f-4-outputs
Host (Claude CLI) down (UI → Host) The operator's request — per the locked schema “the operator asks through the UI; the request runs on the Host (Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one)”. Intent passes down only after Ask-side trust gating (confirm-back / ask-before-acting), with Authority + Tenancy gating every access. section-1-0-runtime
Host (Claude CLI) up (Host → UI) Results back up from the platform run — Orchestration translates and finds the process, System of Process runs it gathering context from System of Record + External World and writes output back to the System of Record; the UI delivers those results to the Human via Receive. The UI carries intent down + results up; it does NOT think. section-1-0-runtime

Example traversal — B1 as a vertical slice through the horizontal blocks

One pillar = one vertical slice through the horizontal blocks. Strong blue boxes = B1 PRIMARY blocks (UI, Orchestration); solid boxes = supporting; dashed boxes = neighbor (External World) and substrate (Compute); the dashed gray container groups Orchestration + System of Process as the Daemon, and both the Daemon container and the Host carry their own runs-on edge to Compute; the dashed purple wrap is the Authority + Tenancy boundary — rendered as a gate on edges, never as a pipeline box. Wrap scope here intentionally differs from the §1.0 drawing: §1.0 encloses the entire pipeline including External World and Compute inside the wrap; this traversal view draws External World and Compute outside it and marks the gate at the boundary-crossing edge instead.

Orchestration

The §1.0 Orchestration block, fully wired · translate the request · find the process

WORKING DRAFT 2026-06-11 — not locked; founder review pending.

First layer: Human → UI → Host → Daemon{ Orchestration (here) → System of Process } → SoR / External World · back to first-layer schema (sec.1.0)

Contracts — edges crossing the block boundary

NeighborDirectionPayloadSource
Hostin Operator request dispatched into the Daemon ("the request runs on the Host; inside the always-on Daemon, Orchestration translates the request"). Arrives as freeform utterance via Day-1 modalities — text chat, Slack mention, email forward, scheduled prompt (voice + screenshot future, per §2.F.B.1). §1.0 diagram edge Host→Daemon is labeled "runs". section-1-0-runtime
Hostout Confirm-back / ASK BACK to the operator when confidence is below threshold (§2.F.B.4–B.5): clarification question ("you mean the Paisa partner email, not the Testlify one?"), operator answers, flow resumes. B1 interaction strip also shows a schema preview returned before execution ({ metrics:[], sources:[], format:one-pager }). section-2-f-2-input-intent
System of Processout The selected process — §1.0 edge label "task = process". Carried content per B1 acceptance: parsed intent_type + structured sub-intents (typed intent), against a pre-declared output schema. System of Process then runs it as THE runtime. section-1-0-runtime

Internal parts

PartWhatDoc home
Request translator First half of the block's stated job: "Orchestration translates the request" (§1.0 prose; SVG sub-label "translate · find the process"). Turns the operator's ask, as dispatched by the Host, into something the Daemon can act on. Rendered above as the group frame holding the intent machinery. section-1-0-runtime
Process finder Second half of the stated job: "finds the relevant process to run". Output edge to System of Process is labeled "task = process" in the §1.0 diagram — the found process IS the task handed down. section-1-0-runtime
Intent Layer (utterance parser) Per B1 Ring 2 contract: parses freeform utterance into typed intent + sub-intents; pre-declared output schema shown before the LLM call; post-LLM output validated and rejected if non-conforming; returns parsed intent_type + structured sub-intents within one turn. Also: show-by-example (operator demonstrates once, Native re-runs) and trusted-names shortcut. pillar-b1
Intent classifier (5 types) Per §2.F.B.2: every utterance resolves to one of five intent types — Task, Query, Directive, Feedback, New Idea — each distinguished by a 'tell' (e.g. "from now on" = Directive; references prior output = Feedback; "what if" = New Idea). section-2-f-2-input-intent
Multi-turn context carry Per §2.F.B.3, five things persist across turns/sessions to resolve references: recent intent (last 3-5 asks), named entities, active charter (standing directives), deferred follow-ups (re-enter context at the right moment), and open questions Native asked but the operator hasn't answered. section-2-f-2-input-intent
Confidence gate (confirm-back) Per §2.F.B.4, four confidence bands: High → proceed (confidence shown only on request); Medium (object ambiguous) → yes/no confirm then proceed; Low (intent unclear) → "I read this as a task — is that right?"; Very low → "act, answer, or remember?". Operator is never surprised by what Native thought they meant. section-2-f-2-input-intent
Intent → journey fan-out router Per §2.F.B.5 diagram: INTENT READ → confidence check → fan-out of the typed intent to its journey (TASK→§C.1, QUERY→§C.2, DIRECTIVE→§C.3, FEEDBACK→§C.4, NEW IDEA→§C.5, UNKNOWN→ASK BACK). section-2-f-2-input-intent
Trigger intake (utterance · cadence · event) Per §4.1 Trigger primitive: what fires a Workflow is an operator utterance, cadence (cron), or event; §4.0 daemon fires triggers on schedule and dispatches matching Workflows. Scheduled prompts are also a Day-1 input modality (§2.F.B.1), so clocked input enters the same translate/find-the-process path. section-4-1-primitives

How a request moves through the block

An operator request enters from the Host (freeform utterance arriving via the §2.F.B.1 modalities, or a scheduled/cadence/event Trigger fired by the daemon). The Intent Layer parses the utterance into typed intent + sub-intents against a pre-declared output schema (post-LLM validated, non-conforming output rejected); the intent classifier resolves it to one of five types (Task/Query/Directive/Feedback/New Idea) using its 'tell', drawing on the multi-turn context carry (recent intents, named entities, active charter, deferred follow-ups, open questions) to resolve references like "do that again for the other client". The result hits the confidence gate: high confidence proceeds; medium/low/very-low loops a confirm-back question out through the Host to the operator before proceeding. The fan-out router then maps the typed intent to its journey, and the process finder selects the relevant process — handed down to System of Process on the "task = process" edge. Authority + Tenancy gates every access along the way.

Open / gaps — questions for the founder

  1. Doc never explicitly assigns the B1 Intent Layer + §2.F.B intent machinery to the Orchestration block — is §1.0's "translates the request" exactly this machinery, or does part of intent parsing run on the Host? (B1 Ring 3 marks the Phase 2 intent-layer-design.md as pending, T-G3-01.)
  2. "Finds the relevant process" has no named mechanism in the cited ranges — how is a typed intent matched to a process/Workflow (registry lookup? Trigger-spec match?), and how do the §C.1–C.5 journeys of the B.5 fan-out map to processes?
  3. Where does the B.3 multi-turn context persist — System of Record, the daemon's ~/.sutra-native/ user-kit, or a state envelope? The cited ranges don't locate it.
  4. B1 Ring 3 says intake artifacts write to B5.a (active session) — does Orchestration write to System of Record directly, or does that write route through System of Process? (Working assumption from founder session: only SoP writes back to SoR — §1.0 shows SoP writing output to SoR but states no exclusivity. Confirm and write down, or drop.)
  5. Do cadence/cron + event Triggers route through Orchestration's translate/find step, or does the daemon dispatch matching Workflows straight to System of Process? §4.0 only says the daemon "handles workflow dispatches + cadence ticks".
  6. The §1.0 diagram has no upward return edge from Orchestration to Host — the transport path for the B.4 confirm-back question is unspecified.
System of Process

WORKING DRAFT 2026-06-11 — not locked; founder review pending.

The §1.0 System of Process block, fully wired · every part connected to the parts it actually touches

First layer: Human → UI → Host → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process (here)} → SoR / External World · first-layer schema (sec.1.0)

How a run flows

  1. Orchestration hands in task = process. The daemon substrate dispatches the matching Workflow (Trigger fire: utterance, cron cadence, or event) under an ACTIVE Charter — the Charter reached ACTIVE earlier via operator approval of PROPOSED (§4.2); new tasks arrive only while the Charter is already ACTIVE.
  2. An agent materializes scoped to that Charter, with inherited HOW context from the method-routing bridge.
  3. Steps execute in declared order (the Workflow declares "ordered Steps"; whether execution is strictly one-at-a-time vs parallel-capable is not stated in the doc) — each Step is an LLM call to the Host or a deterministic action, wrapped in pre-declared-output + post-call check — while the typed state envelope carries turn state across pillar stages (intent → context_envelope → decision_trail → outbound → b5_writes), with Native enforcing shape + authority on every handoff.
  4. Before any conclusion, the test-before-conclude gate runs the tier-appropriate pre-declared test: pass routes to PERSISTED (artifact + triple-channel audit written to System of Record, output presented); fail loops back to B3 retry up to N times per Charter, then ESCALATED through the Approval primitive / Authority gate to the operator.
  5. External-effect calls (send / write / delete / pay) exit via connectors only after the Authority gate; connector half-failures push the run into DEGRADED with reconciliation paths.
  6. Hardstops HS-1..HS-8 can drop the runtime into PAUSED at any point.
  7. CSM sits alongside as the lifecycle ledger of which runtime capabilities are live.
  8. Operator feedback post-conclude feeds B5.d learned patterns that adapt future test thresholds.

Contracts with neighbor blocks

NeighborDirectionPayloadSource
Orchestrationintask = process: Orchestration translates the operator request and finds the relevant process; hands the process to the runtime to run (SVG edge label "task = process"). At runtime granularity this arrives as a Workflow dispatch on Trigger fire (operator utterance, cadence cron, or event).section-1-0-runtime
System of Recordbidirectionalcontext + output (SVG edge label): reads operator context (context_envelope my_own; user-kit markdown Charter/Domain/Workflow specs as authoritative state per ADR-008); writes back artifacts + ExecutionResults + b5_writes (session refs, provenance rows, learned-pattern updates) + triple-channel audit (DecisionProvenance JSONL gate-log/provenance/artifact).section-1-0-runtime
External Worldbidirectionalcontext + effects (SVG edge label) via the Connector layer for any out-of-process tool call: outbound external-effect calls (send / write / delete / pay) each pass the Authority gate; inbound responses return into envelope outbound[].inbound_responses; connector half-failures drop the workflow into DEGRADED.section-4-native-platform
Host (Claude CLI)outLLM calls at Step granularity: each Step is pre-declared expected output + LLM call OR deterministic action + post-call check (P2 pre/post-LLM-validation). The Step primitive (sec.4.1) is the doc's only LLM-call site, which implies System of Process is the Host caller; an explicit only-SoP-calls-Host rule is not yet stated anywhere in the doc — see open question below.section-4-1-primitives
Authority + TenancygatesGates every access: Authority gate fires at every external-effect call (send / write / delete / pay); escalations captured as Approval primitive (pending state + operator verdict + audit row); cross-tenant default-deny with explicit delegates_to edge required (HS-3 + ADR-006); cross-tenant audit queries also default-deny.section-4-4-acceptance
Observability (§7, cross-cutting)outAccuracy KPI (% of outputs passing first-time test vs needing retry); daemon health surfaced only via Daily Pulse health row; audit replay surfaces; platform-health instrumentation ("Depends on … Observability (instruments platform health)" per the §4 header table).section-4-5-test-before-conclude

The eleven internal parts

PartWhatDoc home
Runtime daemon substratePer-operator local background daemon the runtime lives in: home dir ~/.sutra-native/ (user-kit charters/domains/triggers/workflows, decision-provenance.jsonl, native.log); lifecycle START → RUNNING → STOP → RESTART with state restored from user-kit + provenance log; cadence cron ticks fire Triggers and dispatch matching Workflows (ADR-017); re-exec across plugin updates without state loss; per-Tenant isolated boundary (HS-3 + ADR-006).section-4-0-daemon
10 typed primitivesDomain, Charter, Workflow, Step, Trigger, ExecutionResult, EngineEvent (26-event catalog), Tenant, Approval, DecisionProvenance. Step is where work happens: pre-declared expected output + LLM call OR deterministic action + post-call check (P2 pre/post-LLM-validation). Explicitly NOT primitives anymore: Agent (composed from Workflow + Step + Charter scope + Authority gate), Method routing (per-Step config + HOW Engine), Audit substrate (replaced by DecisionProvenance + 3-channel ADR-013).section-4-1-primitives
Workforce deployment state machineHappy path: PROPOSED (operator adds Charter) → ACTIVE (Charter live; new task T0–T3 arrives) → agent materializes scoped to Charter → task completes → PERSISTED (artifact + audit row, output to B5.a–d) → RETIRED (operator revokes / scope expires / superseded; audit replayable forever).section-4-2-state-machine
State envelopeTyped envelope carried across pillar-to-pillar handoffs; Native enforces shape + authority. Fields: turn_id, operator_id, charter_ref, task_tier (T0–T3), intent (from B1), context_envelope (from B2: my_own / worlds_best / peer_sutras_v2), decision_trail (from B3: phase, factors, authority_verdict, output_ref), outbound (from B4: audience, channel, draft, sent_status, inbound_responses), b5_writes (active_session_ref, provenance_row_ref, learned_pattern_updates). Must round-trip with zero data loss or HS-4 fires.section-4-3-state-envelope
Test-before-conclude gate (verify)Accuracy primitive — task never marked done before testing output. 5 steps: (1) pre-declare verification per task tier (T0 grep/file-exists/contract-match; T1 step-by-step + integration check; T2 operator-visible 1-min iteration; T3 milestone-gate); (2) B3 generates output; (3) run pre-declared test (determinism: same input + same Charter → same expected output shape); (4) pass → conclude + audit row + present; fail → loop to B3 retry, max N per Charter, then escalate to operator via Authority gate; (5) operator feedback (drop/accept/refine) becomes B5.d test signal; thresholds adapt per workflow.section-4-5-test-before-conclude
HOW / method-routing bridgeEvery primitive embeds a HOW choice from §3 doctrine: Charters declare HOW preferences at creation; agents materialize with HOW context inherited; method routing (D.11) implements HOW-tier mapping (different problem classes get different solving strategies, per B3b DO); authority gate (D.6) defers to HOW escalation logic. Runtime executes; §3 is the doctrine.section-4-6-how-bridge
Triple-channel audit writes100% of decisions written to triple-channel (gate-log + provenance + artifact); DecisionProvenance JSONL + stderr beacon + tmp fallback (HS-4 + ADR-013); cross-tenant queries default-deny; operator can replay any decision end-to-end via B5.c but never sees raw JSONL.section-4-4-acceptance
Workforce-output delivery capabilityWorkforce produces software + agentic systems, not just text/decisions. Three destinations: D1 shipped-to-client (client owns; Native deletes its copy post-handoff), D2 shipped-back-to-Sutra-org (ratified into plugin/platform for fleet), D3 operator-internal tool. All deliverables emit DecisionProvenance + D.12 artifact-governance metadata (version, owner, source-Charter, audit trail). Delivery pipeline itself owned by §6 Distribution; §4.7 declares only the capability.section-4-7-workforce-outputs
Capability Surface Map (CSM)Feature lifecycle for what the runtime can do: cap-NNN PROPOSED → CANDIDATE → DESIGNED → BUILT (under §4.5 verification) → SHIPPING (T1→T4 cadence) → LIVE/STABLE → DEPRECATED. Fleet tiering L0 fleet-shipped / L1 cohort-staged / L2 instance-local. Operator sees via sutra-capability skill + SessionStart banner.section-4-8-csm
Hardstop catalog (HS-1..HS-8)Operator-visible failure behavior when the runtime cannot continue safely: HS-1 reflexive-check (refuse self-modification), HS-2 governance-overhead >25% token budget (abort turn), HS-3 tenant-boundary (deny cross-tenant without delegates_to), HS-4 audit-unwritable across all 3 channels (full stop — no silent unaudited execution), HS-5 cutover-rollback-fails (pause + HITL), HS-6 3+ governance hooks fail in one turn (session suspended), HS-7 codex-queue-stale (shipments blocked), HS-8 canary-regression (auto-rollback). UX contract: every hardstop emits what happened + recommended next action + audit-row link. Doc flags HS-4..HS-8 as doctrine, mechanism pending (detectors not yet in shipped runtime); HS-1..HS-3 partially enforced.section-4-10-1-hardstops
Failure statesRuntime extension of the §4.2 happy path: PAUSED (hardstop fired or Charter Authority needs operator decision; exit via operator decide → ACTIVE or RETIRED), ESCALATED (B3 retry budget exhausted or Authority ESCALATE class; operator gets B3a THINK 3-angle brainstorm + recommended angle; exit via confirm → ACTIVE with B3b auto-execute, or RETIRED), DEGRADED (connector half-fail / partial side-effect, e.g. send succeeded but audit write missed; exit via idempotent-retry or manual-log-write reconciliation → ACTIVE or PAUSED).section-4-10-2-failure-states

System of Record

The §1.0 System of Record block, fully wired · every part connected to the parts it actually touches

WORKING DRAFT 2026-06-11 — not locked; founder review pending. Standalone source: _block-system-of-record.html.

First layer: Human → UI → Host → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process} → SoR (here) / External World · first-layer schema (sec.1.0)

How state flows

  1. Writes enter from System of Process as the per-turn 5-artifact trail: intent record → context manifest → decision provenance → outbound log → substrate write.
  2. The substrate write lands in B5.a active session, plus learned-pattern updates in B5.d.
  3. The four stores B5.a–d are parallel time horizons, not a graduation pipeline — the PERSISTED step writes output to B5.a/b/c/d simultaneously. B5.a (minutes/hours) backs session continuity with handoff of open threads; B5.b portfolio in-flight (hours/days) holds multi-task state across N companies and survives restart.
  4. B5.c accumulates the append-only DecisionProvenance rows forever — 3-channel per ADR-007; HS-4 fires on triple-channel write failure or on any silent overwrite.
  5. B5.d distills learned patterns (months/forever, k=4 emergence). Together with the E.2/E.3 memory layers (working memory, named entities, trusted names, prior intents, ratified directives) and charter preferences, these are what B2 reads at intake and B1 reads for shorthand on future turns.
  6. The versioned artifact store (D.12) cross-links to B5.c: every artifact write produces a DecisionProvenance row, references resolve to exact versions, and changes trigger Connector notifications outward.
  7. Telemetry lands via the JSONL + fsync sink (ADR-013). The §1.0 diagram splits SoR contents as "own | external". Every read and write across all parts is gated by Authority + Tenancy with cross-tenant default-deny.

Contracts — neighbors

NeighborDirectionPayloadSource
System of Process bidirectional IN (context reads): B2 reads at intake (ranked context retrieval); B1 reads for shorthand interpretation; learned patterns + standing prefs shape future iterations. OUT-bound from SoP (writes): every pillar B1–B4 writes here — per-turn 5-artifact audit trail, versioned output artifacts, DecisionProvenance row per decision/artifact-write, substrate write (active-session ref + learned-pattern updates). Diagram edge label: "context + output". section-1-0-runtime
Authority + Tenancy gates Gates every access (cross-cutting wrap). Tenant-scoped: cross-tenant queries default-deny (B5 acceptance + §7.1 verified metric); B5 crosscut: whose data lives where + who can read across tenant boundaries; operator's portfolio is the boundary unit, not the company. Tenant isolation via Domain field on every primitive (ADR-006). pillar-b5
Observability (§7) out Audit + replay surfaces: all 5 per-turn artifacts replayed to operator on demand ("replay last Paisa turn" → 5 artifacts chronologically); replay ≤30s for a single-day window; learned-pattern diffs ("what changed in B5.d this week"); B5 drift signals (replay latency >60s OR any cross-tenant leak). section-7-2-audit
Connector (§5) out Notify-on-change: when an artifact shared with audience X changes, §5 Connector notifies audience X (Slack/email per Charter) within 60s of change. d12-artifact-governance
Operator (via UI→Host→Daemon runtime path) bidirectional IN: operator writes standing prefs directly (B5 Ring 3); charter entries (E.2); forget / correct / pin-as-durable commands (E.5, honored immediately + visibly confirmed). OUT: curation surface — "what do you remember about X?" returns structured view of entities, prior intents, standing directives, charter entries touching X. section-2-f-5-personalization-memory

Internal parts

PartWhatDoc home
B5.a Active session store Minutes/hours horizon. What's right-now in flight. Backs N22 session-continuity. b5a-active
B5.b Portfolio in-flight state Hours/days horizon. Multi-task state for the 5 in-flight tasks across N companies; survives session restart. Backs N24. Token-budget allocation + multi-thread state machine deferred to Phase 2 design. b5b-portfolio
B5.c Audit + replay Days/forever horizon. DecisionProvenance + AUDIT surface. Backs N#8. Acceptance: 100% of decisions carry a DecisionProvenance row; replay any task end-to-end in ≤30s for a single-day window. b5c-audit
B5.d Learned patterns Months/forever horizon. Person Formation + pattern emergence (k=4). Backs N#5. Learned patterns shape future iterations; operator standing prefs land here and future B2 reads them. b5d-learned
Memory layers (E.3) + forget rules (E.4) Five layers with distinct lifespans: working memory (session, handoff if thread open) · named entities (until forget or 90-day inactive + confirmed archive) · trusted names (durable, change needs explicit confirmation) · prior intents (rolling, older entries summarize) · ratified directives (until revoked). Forgets: one-time sensitive content, expired time-bounded charters, anything operator asks to forget (immediate + visibly confirmed). section-2-f-5-personalization-memory
Skill modes (E.1) Per-domain skill-mode map (SM1 novice / SM2 intermediate / SM3 expert) — how Native talks to this operator. Inferred from phrasing/corrections or set explicitly; per-domain, not global. Separate mechanism from the E.2 charter. section-2-f-5-personalization-memory
Charter & declared preferences (E.2) What the operator explicitly told Native about how to be served — cadence, hands-off level per ask class, communication style, channel defaults, people & relationships. Durable until revoked. section-2-f-5-personalization-memory
Per-turn 5-artifact audit trail Every Native turn produces 5 stored artifacts: 1. intent record (B1) · 2. context manifest with ranked artifact list + what was dropped (B2) · 3. decision provenance with phases + factors + authority verdict + output (B3) · 4. outbound log (B4, if comms) · 5. substrate write (B5: active-session ref + learned-pattern updates). section-7-2-audit
Versioned artifact store (D.12 lifecycle integrity) Append-not-overwrite: every change adds a version, prior version stays accessible; reference-with-version (artifact@v3 resolves exactly); metadata (owner, version, source-of-truth status) travels with the artifact; every artifact write produces a DecisionProvenance row linking creator-Charter + reason + diff-from-prior. Zero silent overwrites (HS-4 fires on version-bump-less replace). d12-artifact-governance
DecisionProvenance row schema (ADR-007) 3-channel append-only audit row per decision (ratified). HS-4 fires on triple-channel write failure (per B5 acceptance). section-12-1-adr-table
Telemetry sink (ADR-013) JSONL + fsync as telemetry sink — no silent loss (ratified). section-12-1-adr-table

Authority + Tenancy

The §1.0 Authority + Tenancy wrap, fully wired · every part connected to the parts it actually touches

First layer: Human → UI → Host → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process} → SoR / External World — Authority + Tenancy (here) is the wrap that gates every access between them · back to first-layer schema (sec.1.0)

Contracts — what crosses this block's boundary

ALL block pairs (Human · UI · Host · Daemon · System of Record · External World · Compute) gates Every access. §1.0 draws Authority + Tenancy as a dashed wrap around the entire runtime ("AUTHORITY + TENANCY · gates every access") — a cross-cutting envelope, not an in-line stage on any single edge. section-1-0-runtime

(first-time channel) — plus reversibility tag on every action and audit-replay answers (F.3). HS-1 refuses workflow self-modification of Sutra core primitives without direct ratification. section-4-10-2-failure-states

Internal parts — what lives inside the wrap

PartWhatDoc home
Authority Model — 6 gate dimensions (D.6) Six dimensions decide auto-execute vs ask-operator: by domain (per-Charter company scope), by tool (Slack yes, bank-API no without auth), by stakeholder (team auto, board/regulator escalate), by dollar threshold (≤$X auto, >$X ask), by reversibility (reversible auto, irreversible ask), by confidence level (high auto, low ask). Applies cross-pillar, heaviest in B3 action gate + B4 delegation. d6-authority
Ask-vs-act decision cascade (F.1 + F.5) Ordered 4-question cascade per action: irreversible? → ASK multi-step confirm ("type go"); high-risk (spend/send/share/commit touching people or money)? → ASK one-tap confirm; new authority (no prior permission template)? → ASK grant once / grant always / not now; first time on channel/contact? → ASK preview-before-send. All no → ACT, report after. section-2-f-6-trust-authority
Reversibility tags (F.2) Every action carries a visible reversibility tag: Reversible (undo window, "say undo anytime"), Reversible with effort (retract/re-issue/re-pay — doable, not free), Not reversible (confirming locks it in). section-2-f-6-trust-authority
Trust ladder (F.4) Autonomy earned over time, 4 rungs: Rung 1 (day 0-14) ask before every external action; Rung 2 (15-45) auto-act on repeated patterns, ask on novel; Rung 3 (46-90) auto-act on routines, ask only high-risk/irreversible; Rung 4 (90+) runs standing routines, surfaces what it did. Operator can dial up/down explicitly at any rung. section-2-f-6-trust-authority
Approval-gate primitive (ADR-009) Approval is a first-class primitive in the platform, not a hook (ADR-009, ratified). Approval plumbing lives in canon blocks/ + primitives/ per the §1.5 Governance Machinery row. section-12-1-adr-table
Tenant isolation via Domain field (ADR-006) Tenant isolation enforced via a Domain field on every primitive (ADR-006, ratified). §1.3 names Identity/Tenancy as a cross-cut: implicit in D.6 by-domain, explicit cross-tenant default-deny in §2 B5. section-12-1-adr-table
HS-3 tenant-boundary hardstop Execution attempting cross-tenant access without an explicit delegates_to edge is DENIED; operator gets "this action would touch <other-company>; explicit delegates_to authorization required" prompt. Per the MECHANISM-PENDING callout, HS-3 has only partial enforcement via existing plugin hooks today. section-4-10-1-hardstops
Hardstop UX contract Every hardstop produces (a) 1-2 sentence operator-facing WHAT-happened message, (b) recommended NEXT ACTION (decide / authorize / wait / investigate), (c) link to the audit row. Operator never reads engineer-level stack traces. HS-1 reflexive-check additionally refuses self-modification of Sutra core rules until operator ratifies directly. section-4-10-1-hardstops
Authority-driven failure states (PAUSED / ESCALATED) PAUSED entered when a hardstop fires OR Charter Authority requires explicit operator decision (per D.6); exit = operator decides → ACTIVE or RETIRED. ESCALATED entered when retry budget exhausts OR Authority gate triggers ESCALATE class (ADR-018 contract 1: AUTO/GATE/ESCALATE classification); exit = operator confirms direction. section-4-10-2-failure-states
Audit visibility / replay surface (F.3) Operator can ask Native to replay any past action at any moment: what was asked, what Native did, why it chose that path, what it would do differently. No log-diving or IT tickets — same chat surface. section-2-f-6-trust-authority
Governance machinery guard tooling Live action-time guards enforcing the authority + audit + intervention contract as HARD/SOFT blocks (canon write-lock, dispatcher hooks, build-layer check, tenant firewall, etc.). HARD fire = boxed BLOCKED message with rule cited + override path (env-var ACK, honor-system, audit-logged); SOFT = advisory, tool call proceeds. The §1.5 row states every decision carries a provenance row and cross-tenant is default-deny. section-1-5-1-governance-machinery

How it flows internally

Every access entering the wrap is classified by the D.6 six-dimension Authority Model (domain · tool · stakeholder · dollar · reversibility · confidence), then run through the F.5 ask-vs-act cascade (irreversible → high-risk → new-authority → first-time-channel → else ACT-and-report); any ASK outcome is realized by the approval-gate primitive (ADR-009), which surfaces the prompt up through UI carrying its F.2 reversibility tag, while the gated workflow inside System of Process holds in PAUSED or ESCALATED until the operator's decision flows back down. In parallel, the tenancy half checks every System-of-Record touch against the ADR-006 Domain field, with HS-3 default-denying cross-tenant access lacking an explicit delegates_to edge; denials surface via the hardstop UX contract (WHAT + next action + audit-row link). Operator responses ("grant always", dial up/down) feed the F.4 trust ladder, which shifts future cascade outcomes from ask toward auto over rungs 1-4; every decision through the gate writes a DecisionProvenance row, which the F.3 replay surface reads back on demand, and the §1.5.1 guard tooling enforces the same contract at action-time as HARD/SOFT blocks.

Open / gaps — questions for the founder

  • Where does the gate physically execute per edge — the §1.0 SVG wraps all blocks (incl. Human→UI and Host), but the cited ranges only name enforcement at System of Process execution, connector sends, and SoR tenancy; is UI→Host / Host→Daemon gated by the same mechanism?
  • Is Orchestration's process-selection step itself authority-gated, or does gating only begin once System of Process executes?
  • F.1 "grant always" implies a persisted permission template — where is it stored (Charter? System of Record?) and what schema?
  • Which block owns trust-ladder state (current rung / operator dial), and is it scoped per-operator, per-Charter, or per-tenant?
  • HS-3 tenant-boundary is flagged MECHANISM-PENDING-adjacent ("partial enforcement via existing plugin hooks") — what exactly is enforced live today vs doctrine-only?
  • Does Authority + Tenancy say anything about the Compute layer boundary? Per-tenant key/data isolation appears only in the deferred cloud-hosted channel activation gate (§1.4.5), not for the v1 own-machine case.
  • Where does the DecisionProvenance audit trail live in the first-layer schema — inside System of Record or a separate store? The cited ranges name the row contract (ADR-007) but not its home block.
Host (Claude CLI)

The §1.0 Host block, fully wired · every part connected to what it actually touches

WORKING DRAFT 2026-06-11 — not locked; founder review pending. Standalone source: _block-host.html.

First layer: Human → UI → Host (here) → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process} → SoR / External World

Internal flow

The operator request enters from the UI into the Host's agent runtime; runtime and model are one fused unit (single block, no internal edge to a separate LLM box — the doc explicitly collapses them: “Claude CLI — agent runtime + model in one”). The Host then carries the request downward into the Daemon on the edge labeled “runs”, where Orchestration translates it and selects the process. Daemon lifecycle (session-boot start or explicit sutra native start, §4.0) is attributed by §4.0 to the Sutra plugin (“managed by plugin”), now placed Host-resident per ADR-021 (gap #4 closed) — the lifecycle edge is drawn from the Host only on the partial support of §1.0's “runs” label. A return edge comes back up from System of Process: when a process runs, it invokes a host LLM selected per workflow among claude/codex/deepseek (ADR-005) — so the Host slot is entered from below as well as above. Everything sits inside the Authority + Tenancy gate wrap and stands on the Compute base layer.

Open / gaps — questions for the founder

This exclusivity appears nowhere in index.html — §1.0 states no such rule, and ADR-005 only says host-LLM selection is per-workflow. Confirm and write it down (§1.0 or an ADR), or drop it.
  • ADR-005 allows codex/deepseek as host-LLMs, yet §1.0 labels the Host box “Claude CLI” singular — is Claude CLI the fixed operator-facing host while per-workflow LLM calls may route to a different host, or can the top-of-stack Host itself be swapped? Cited ranges do not reconcile this.

  • Host is per-session while Daemon is “always-on” — what happens to an in-flight operator request if the Host (Claude CLI session) exits while the Daemon is still RUNNING? Cited ranges cover daemon restart but not Host-absence semantics.
  • No internal decomposition of the Host beyond “agent runtime + model in one” exists anywhere in the cited ranges — is second-order detail for the Host block (session state, tool loop, context window) intentionally deferred to the UI/_consumer-product detailing pass?

Host machine (execution substrate) The base layer of the §1.0 runtime diagram: a single dashed box labeled “COMPUTE · host machine — where the Host + daemon execute”. The doc defines it only as the machine on which Host (Claude CLI) and Daemon run; v1 compute is per-operator and local (“Native runs as a per-operator local daemon”). section-1-0-runtime
Per-operator filesystem state (~/.sutra-native/) Compute-resident daemon root holding user-kit (charters, domains, triggers, workflows), decision-provenance.jsonl, native.log; plus the 3-channel audit persistence (DecisionProvenance JSONL + stderr beacon + tmp fallback) and user-kit markdown as authoritative state (ADR-008). Documented under §4.0 as the daemon’s footprint on the host machine; not operator-visible. section-4-0-daemon
OS process facility (single daemon process) Single background daemon process per operator, started by Sutra plugin at session boot or explicit “sutra native start”; re-exec across plugin updates without state loss; lifecycle START (plugin launch / cron / cold boot) → RUNNING → STOP → RESTART (state restored from user-kit + decision-provenance log). section-4-0-daemon
Cron / scheduling facility Host-machine cadence source: per-Workflow trigger spec with cadence field (ADR-017 cron-daemon-cadence-tick); daemon fires triggers on schedule. Cron is also one of the three daemon START paths (plugin launch / cron / cold boot). section-4-0-daemon
Cloud-hosted compute (deferred, v2+) Managed-infra distribution channel where the operator does not run their own daemon. Deferred behind activation gates: multi-tenant infra spec + HS-3 tenant-boundary extension to shared-host, per-tenant data-residency + key-isolation, pricing tier, cloud-managed install path. Until then, Compute = operator-local machine only. section-1-4-5-activation

Internal flow (as documented)

The doc draws Compute as one undivided dashed box at the base of the §1.0 diagram; all internal structure above is assembled from the daemon’s documented footprint in §4.0, not from a Compute-section decomposition. On the host machine, the OS process facility runs the single per-operator daemon process; the cron facility feeds the process facility twice (as a START trigger alongside plugin launch and cold boot, and as recurring cadence ticks that the daemon turns into Workflow dispatches per ADR-017); the process facility reads and writes the per-operator filesystem state at ~/.sutra-native/ continuously (audit JSONL writes + user-kit markdown while RUNNING) and reads it back on RESTART (state restored from user-kit + decision-provenance log, surviving plugin re-exec without state loss).

The Host (Claude CLI) executes on the same machine, but the doc draws no compute-internal edge between Host and the daemon at this layer — their interaction is modeled above Compute in the §1.0 stack. The deferred cloud-hosted variant (v2+) would replace the operator-local machine entirely, with no schema impact described yet.

Open / gaps — questions for the founder

  • No hardware/OS requirements for Compute anywhere in the doc (supported OS, CPU/memory/disk minimums) — what does an operator’s machine need?
  • Is Host + Daemon co-location on one machine a hard constraint, or can they split across machines? §1.0 only says both “run on the Compute”.
  • Compute failure semantics beyond “cold boot → START”: what happens to in-flight Workflow executions when the machine sleeps, crashes, or loses power mid-run?
  • Cloud-hosted channel (§1.4.5) defers managed compute to v2+ but never says how the §1.0 schema changes when compute is not operator-local — where does the Host (Claude CLI) run in that channel?
  • No resource governance on the operator’s machine: nothing caps daemon CPU/disk usage or defines retention/rotation for decision-provenance.jsonl and native.log growth.
  • Authority + Tenancy “gates every access” wraps the Compute box in the diagram, but no compute-level enforcement mechanism (disk encryption, key storage location, local access control) is named.
External World

The §1.0 External World block, fully wired · every counterparty connected over its own Connector channel

WORKING DRAFT 2026-06-11 — not locked; founder review pending. Standalone source: _block-external-world.html.

First layer: first-layer schema (sec.1.0) · Human → UI → Host → Daemon{Orchestration → System of Process} → SoR / External World (here)

Internal flow

The parts of the External World do not connect to each other — they are parallel counterparties, each reached over its own Connector channel. Traffic flows in two one-way lanes through the Connector transport (§5.2): on the outbound lane, System of Process output (B3's decision, B4's draft) passes the Authority gate, becomes a typed envelope with retry logic, and lands on a service endpoint (Slack, email, web, 3rd-party tool) or, through Slack/email, on a human stakeholder; on the inbound lane, a human's reply (e.g. the board chair's Slack response) is identity-verified, wrapped into a typed envelope, and routed back to B3 as the next action — so people and services only "meet" inside System of Process, never inside the External World block. Peer Sutras have only a log-only A2A stub edge in v1 (no real transport), and browser/phone counterparts have no live edge at all. At the §1.0 altitude the whole block collapses to a single bidirectional "context + effects" edge with System of Process, with Authority + Tenancy wrapping every crossing.

Contracts — neighbor edges

NeighborDirectionPayloadSource
System of Process bidirectional "context + effects" (§1.0 edge label): System of Process gathers context FROM the External World and pushes effects INTO it; this is one of the two external edges of the runtime (the other being System of Record, which gets "context + output"). section-1-0-runtime
System of Process in Outbound sends via the Connector outbound lane (B3 → B4): audience-calibrated draft converted to a typed envelope, Authority-gated at every send, retry logic on transient fail, DecisionProvenance row per send; permanent failures surface to operator — no silent loss. section-5-2-flow
System of Process out Inbound receives via the Connector inbound lane (B4 → B3): identity-verified, payload converted to a typed envelope, routed to B3 as next action; inbound from unverified identity logs + drops and never enters B3. Inbound responses re-enter the loop via B3 and prior comms become B2 future context. section-5-2-flow
Authority + Tenancy gates Every outbound call passes the Authority gate with default-deny on missing scope; per-tool Charter scoping enforced (e.g. "Slack:#paisa-team only", not "Slack:*"); B4 adds per-stakeholder + tool + $-threshold gating on who Native may talk to. section-5-3-acceptance

Internal parts

PartWhatDoc home
People (operator's stakeholders) Humans Native talks to on the operator's behalf — board / founder / team / client / vendor, each with audience-calibrated voice per the CX Bar overlay. Doc's worked example: board chair receives an audience-calibrated Slack DM and replies inbound ("got it; let's discuss Friday"). pillar-b4
External services Service endpoints reached via Connector transport: Slack (in + out, Live), Email via Resend (out, Live — daily pulse + notifications), Web fetch/search (out, Live — feeds B2.b world's-best context), 3rd-party tools (out, pluggable via Composio with per-tool Charter scoping). §5.2 names them collectively "THE WORLD (Slack · email · web · 3rd-pty)". section-5-1-inventory
Peer Sutras (peer-Native instances) Other operators' Native instances. v1 status: log-only A2A stub, no real transport. Full peer-Native A2A is v2+, gated per §1.4.5 activation map (B2.c Peer Sutras live first, A2A canon ADR, cross-Native identity/auth, audit extension). section-5-1-inventory
Deferred-channel counterparts (browser · phone) Endpoints listed in the v1 inventory but not reachable yet: Browser co-work session (in + out, Charter TODO-005, deferred to v1.x, spec pending) and Phone voice + SMS humans (not planned v1, v2+ per §1.4.5 phone-connector activation gate). section-5-1-inventory

§1.4 Product Hierarchy — Platform · Product · Module · Deployment

Native + CoS form one cell of a multi-level product hierarchy. Operator-visible child products (Daily Pulse, Feedback Engine, etc.) are Modules. Per-operator + per-Charter installs are Deployments.

   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  LAYER A · PLATFORM                                            │
   │  Native — workforce-deployment substrate                       │

   └────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┘
                                │ hosts
                                ▼
   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  LAYER B · PRODUCT                                             │
   │  Chief of Staff (CoS) — first-party flagship                   │

   └────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┘
                                │ composes
                                ▼
   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  LAYER C · MODULE                                              │
   │  Composed unit on top of CoS — independently deployable        │
   │  (Daily Pulse §7.5 · Feedback Engine §6.6 · Audit Replay ·     │
   │   Workforce Status · Charter Console · ...)                    │
   └────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────────┘
                                │ binds + runs as
                                ▼
   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  LAYER D · DEPLOYMENT                                          │
   │  Per-operator · per-Charter · per-tenant install               │
   │  (e.g., "Daily Pulse for Sankalp's Paisa Charter")             │
   └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

§1.4.1 Module eligibility — strict 4-test rule

A capability becomes a Module only if it satisfies ALL four:

#TestWhat it means
1Independent lifecycleCan be added / updated / retired without touching the host Product's release cycle
2Explicit configuration surfaceHas per-Deployment binding config (not hardcoded into Product code)
3Own runtime state / observabilityProduces its own audit rows, metrics, replay artifacts (not just inherits Product's)
4Possible reuseDesigned so a future second Product could compose it (cross-product reuse designed for, not guaranteed in v1)

If a capability fails any test → it's a feature of Product, not a Module. Avoid Module-bloat (every feature inflated into a Module). Daily Pulse + Feedback Engine pass; small UI panels don't.

§1.4.2 Capability vs Module — distinct concepts

ConceptDefinitionExamples
Capability (cap-NNN)Atomic platform/product primitive · single technical service · tracked in CSM (§4.8)cap-001 Input Routing · cap-002 Depth Estimation · cap-003 Blueprint · cap-007 SBOM
ModulePackaged composition of multiple Capabilities + UX + policy + per-Deployment config + own runtime stateDaily Pulse (composes cap-Telemetry + cap-Audit + cap-CrossPortfolio-Priority) · Feedback Engine (composes cap-Connector-Slack + cap-Privacy-Sanitize + cap-Audit)

Capabilities are building blocks. Modules are deployable units. CSM tracks both: capabilities flat-indexed; modules referenced as compositions.

§1.4.3 Deployment authority — Modules + Layer-B Products (v1 → v1.5 → v2)

Same authority phases apply to BOTH Modules (per §1.4.1) AND Layer-B Products (per §1.4.4 — CoS + future Senior Expert + Project Manager). Asawa/Sutra Forge originates both in v1; operator gets binding-only deployment at v1.5; third-party authoring opens at v2+.

PhaseWho can deploy a ModuleWho can deploy a Layer-B ProductRationale
v1Native platform + Asawa Forge onlyAsawa/Sutra Forge only (CoS is the first; Senior Expert + Project Manager future)Strong governance; no ungoverned automation attached to operator workflows
v1.5Operator self-serve from approved Module catalog (binding-only, not spec-authoring)Operator self-serve from approved Product catalog (binding-only — operator picks which Layer-B Product to deploy under a Charter; cannot author new Products)Operator can bind pre-approved Modules + Products to Charters; cannot author new ones
v2+Third-party developers, gated by review + permissioning + auditThird-party Layer-B Product developers (own pillars + capability surface), gated by review + permissioning + audit + canon-ADR authoringMarketplace; only after Capability/Module/Product review pipeline matures

§1.4.4 Layer-B products on Native (current + planned)

Native (§1.4 Layer A) is a multi-product platform. CoS is one Layer-B product on it; future products sit at the same layer. Each Layer-B product specifies its own pillars + capability surface; all share the same Native Platform substrate + canon.

#Layer-B ProductOperator surfaceStatus
1Chief of Staff (CoS)First-party flagship; operator (per §A.2). Implementation consultant; 5 pillars (§2.B B1-B5).v1 in spec; PRD §2 defines
2Senior ExpertProject executor — autonomously runs an entire project end-to-end under operator-set Charter. Operator-grade decision-maker for a specific domain (legal · finance · engineering · product · etc.).Future; spec TBD
3Project ManagerOperator-facing project orchestration — milestones, dependencies, stakeholder comms, status tracking across multi-week initiatives.Future; spec TBD

Audience model (B2B): every Layer-B product surfaces to users of our product — operators who deploy Native within their org (per §A.2). No B2C / B2B2C surface in v1. Future products beyond CoS may serve operator's team members (peers · reports) but always under operator-Charter authority — never independent of operator.

Activation gate discipline: each deferred capability MUST satisfy all gate rows before flipping live. Operator never wakes up to find a v2 capability live without it having cleared the gates above. Per §C.4 R9 canon-drift discipline: activation is a versioned canon change, codex+deepseek reviewed, audit-trailed.

§1.5 Tech-parts cross-cut overlay

Four technical contract categories cut across every Layer-B product + every CoS pillar. PRD body surfaces what the operator observes (capability contract). Engine architecture — type signatures, state machines, invariants, lifecycle, storage — lives in canon (sutra/os/native/** + ADRs at sutra/os/decisions/). This overlay is the bridge: names what each category MEANS for the operator + where the deep spec lives.

CategoryWhat it is (operator-facing capability)Canon location (engine-facing deep spec)
Domain ModelsOperator-visible accountability model — the entities the operator works with (Charters · Workflows · Artifacts · Decisions). Operator can name them, scope them, audit them.Type signatures + invariants + lifecycle in primitives/ (Domain · Charter · Workflow · DecisionProvenance) + blocks/B10-typed-config.md. PRD never duplicates schemas.
HooksSafeguard + escalation behavior — system catches its own failures and escalates per Charter rules. Operator never debugs the system; the system tells the operator when something needs decision.Hardstop catalog (HS-1 .. HS-8) in hardstops/ + ADRs on failure semantics + recovery contracts. PRD never duplicates hook sets / thresholds / fail modes.
Governance MachineryAuthority + audit + intervention contract — when system asks operator vs auto-executes (per §D.6 Authority Model); every decision carries a provenance row; cross-tenant default-deny (per §A.6).State machine + event catalog + approval plumbing in blocks/ + primitives/ + ADRs 006 (tenant-isolation) · 007 (decision-provenance) · 009 (approval-gate) · 013 (telemetry-sink). PRD never duplicates state machines or audit plumbing.
Agentic-Systems PatternOperator-in-loop on consequential decisions; deterministic guardrails around stochastic reasoning. System is autonomous on known paths, deferential on novel / risky / high-stakes paths.sutra/os/decisions/ADR-018-agentic-systems-pattern.md (anchored by P4 "product-pov-before-tech-pov" + P12 "deterministic-surface-stochastic-core"). PRD never duplicates agent-loop architecture or decision-threshold tuning.

How to use this overlay: when reading any Layer-B product PRD section, these four categories define the SAME contract surface (one product's hooks behave the same way as another's). When changing engine substrate (canon), this overlay is the trace — a change to primitives/ can ripple into operator-visible Domain Models surface. The full design ↔ product ↔ tech traceability table lives in sutra/os/decisions/ADR-019-design-product-tech-bridge.md (engineering audience; not gating reading material).

The Governance Machinery row above lists ADRs + primitives. Below is the live guard tooling that enforces those contracts at action-time — what surfaces to the operator as a HARD/SOFT block. Added 2026-05-13 to close §10.10 (A) #1/2/5/6/7 (codex Lane A flagged this layer as previously invisible).
