§4 Native Platform — workforce-deployment substrate #

PurposeThe substrate that scopes, deploys, and supervises a workforce of agents. CoS (and future products) ride on it.
OwnsCharters (fixed/flex) · Agents (on-demand materialization) · Method routing per task tier · Authority gates · State envelope between pillars · Audit substrate (DecisionProvenance).
Depends on§5 Connector layer (for any out-of-process tool call); §7 Observability (instruments platform health).
Does NOT ownOperator surface; pillar UX; pricing; channels.

Native is the engine room. Operators don't see Native directly — they see CoS (§2). Native shows up only when CoS asks Native for a Charter scope, an agent, a method route, or an authority decision. Detailed primitive definitions live in §9 D.10 (Charter Model) and §9 D.11 (Method Selection); §4 surfaces the runtime behavior + state.

§4.0 Runtime daemon — where Native lives + runs

Native runs as a per-operator local daemon. The operator doesn't see it (per §A.2.2 post-onboarding zero-terminal); the daemon manages workforce deployment, audit-log writes, hook firing, and cadence scheduling silently in the background.

AspectWhatOperator-visible?
Home directory~/.sutra-native/ — per-operator daemon root containing user-kit (charters, domains, triggers, workflows), decision-provenance.jsonl, native.logNo (filesystem internal)
Process modelSingle background daemon process per operator. Started by Sutra plugin at session boot OR by explicit sutra native start. Re-exec across plugin updates without state loss.No (managed by plugin)
LifecycleSTART (plugin launch / cron / cold boot) → RUNNING (handles workflow dispatches + hook firings + cadence ticks + audit writes) → STOP (plugin shutdown / explicit stop) → RESTART (state restored from user-kit + decision-provenance log)Only via Daily Pulse health row (per §7)
State persistence3-channel audit (DecisionProvenance JSONL + stderr beacon + tmp fallback per §4.10 HS-4 + ADR-013); user-kit markdown files for Charter / Domain / Workflow specs (per ADR-008 markdown-vs-code authoritative state)Operator can request audit replay (B5.c); never sees the JSONL directly
Cadence (cron)Per-Workflow trigger spec with cadence field (per ADR-017 cron-daemon-cadence-tick); daemon fires triggers on schedule and dispatches matching WorkflowsOperator sees the SCHEDULED OUTPUT (daily pulse, weekly synthesis, etc.); not the cron firing
BoundaryPer-Tenant isolated (per HS-3 + ADR-006); cross-tenant calls require explicit delegates_to edge; default-deny on cross-tenant data flowOnly visible if HS-3 fires on attempted boundary cross — operator sees "this would touch "

Canon link: full daemon runtime ships at sutra/marketplace/native/bin/sutra-native (CLI launcher) + sutra/marketplace/native/src/ (runtime code · engine · cli · types · runtime · persistence) + ADR-005 host-LLM selection + ADR-013 telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl + ADR-017 cron-daemon-cadence-tick. Path corrected 2026-05-13 per §10.10 P1 #5 (codex Lane A C #3) — shipped runtime lives under sutra/marketplace/native/, not sutra/marketplace/plugin/. Operator-visible health surface lives in §7 Observability.

§4.1 Primitives (10 canon — operator-facing capability + canon link)

Native runtime uses 10 typed primitives (per canon at sutra/os/native/primitives/). PRD body describes the operator-facing capability of each; engine type signatures + invariants + lifecycle live in canon.

PrimitiveOperator-facing capabilityCanon link
DomainOperator-named scope of work (one company / function / project). Each Domain has typed config (principles + guidelines + decisions) — operator declares + edits.primitives/domain.md · blocks/B10-typed-config.md
CharterOperator-set behavioral contract scoping deployed workforce over time. Fixed (rules locked) or flexible (rules adapt with operator approval). Charter is what operator authorizes; everything Native does is under some Charter.primitives/charter.md · §9 D.10 Charter Model
WorkflowMulti-step task spec: ordered Steps + Trigger + on-failure policy. Operator declares Workflows; Native dispatches them on Trigger fire.primitives/workflow.md
StepAtomic unit of a Workflow — pre-declared expected output + LLM call OR deterministic action + post-call check (per P2 pre/post-LLM-validation). Step is where work happens.primitives/step.md · ADR-012 PNC typed parser
TriggerWhat fires a Workflow — operator utterance, cadence (cron), or event. Trigger spec lives in Workflow file.primitives/trigger.md · ADR-017 cron cadence
ExecutionResultWhat a Workflow produced — typed output artifact, status (success / failure / partial / paused), audit row references. Operator-readable summary; system-readable for next iteration (per P1 artifact-first).primitives/execution-result.md
EngineEventTyped event in 26-event catalog (workflow_started · step_complete · approval_requested · etc.). Emitted by daemon during workflow run; consumed by observers + audit.primitives/engine-event.md · 26 event files at events/
TenantIsolation boundary (per company / Charter scope). Every Domain owned by exactly one Tenant. Cross-tenant operations require explicit delegates_to edge (per HS-3 + ADR-006).primitives/tenant.md · ADR-006 tenant-isolation
ApprovalGate primitive — when Workflow Step requires operator decision (per Authority Model D.6 ESCALATE class), Approval primitive captures the pending state + operator's verdict + audit row.primitives/approval.md · ADR-009 approval-gate · §9 D.6 Authority
DecisionProvenanceEvery consequential decision produces one DecisionProvenance row. JSONL audit log + 3-channel fallback per ADR-013. Operator can replay any decision end-to-end via B5.c.primitives/decision-provenance.md · ADR-007 schema · ADR-013 telemetry-sink

What's NOT a primitive (older PRD had these): "Agent" (no longer a typed primitive — agent behavior is composed from Workflow + Step + Charter scope + Authority gate). "Method routing" (handled per-Step config + HOW Engine in §3). "Audit substrate" (replaced by DecisionProvenance primitive + 3-channel ADR-013).

§4.3 State envelope — what flows between pillars

Pillar-to-pillar handoffs carry a typed envelope. CoS's 5 pillars (§2.B) consume + produce envelope fragments; Native enforces shape + authority.

   { turn_id,
     operator_id,
     charter_ref,                   // which Charter scopes this turn
     task_tier,                     // T0/T1/T2/T3
     intent: { type, sub_intents }, // from B1
     context_envelope: {            // from B2
       my_own:[…],
       worlds_best:[…],
       peer_sutras_v2:[…]
     },
     decision_trail: [              // from B3
       { phase, factors, authority_verdict, output_ref }
     ],
     outbound: [                    // from B4 (if comms triggered)
       { audience, channel, draft, sent_status, inbound_responses }
     ],
     b5_writes: {                   // to B5
       active_session_ref,
       provenance_row_ref,
       learned_pattern_updates
     }
   }

§4.4 Acceptance criteria (Native platform v1)

  • Charter create/update/retire is operator-only; agent materialization is Native-internal.
  • Authority gate fires at every external-effect call (send · write · delete · pay).
  • State envelope round-trips with zero data loss across pillar handoffs (HS-4 fires if it doesn't).
  • Audit substrate writes 100% of decisions to triple-channel (gate-log + provenance + artifact); cross-tenant queries default-deny.

§4.5 Test-before-conclude gate (accuracy primitive)

Native does NOT mark a task done before testing the output. Accuracy is a per-workflow KPI. Test happens BEFORE concluding, not after. Output is rejected back to B3 retry if test fails.

StepWhat happens
1 · Pre-declare testFor each task tier, Native pre-declares the verification criteria BEFORE running. For T0 (atomic): grep / file-exists / contract-match. For T1 (multi-action): step-by-step verify + final integration check. For T2 (iterative): operator-visible 1-min iteration verification. For T3 (Charter): milestone-gate verification at goal-transition points.
2 · Generate outputB3 produces output (auto-execute path).
3 · Run testNative runs the pre-declared test against produced output. Determinism: same input + same Charter → same expected output shape.
4 · Conditional concludeIf test passes → conclude + write audit row + present to operator. If test fails → loop to B3 retry (max N retries per Charter); after N failures → escalate to operator (Authority gate).
5 · Post-concludeOperator's feedback (drop / accept / refine) becomes test signal for B5.d learned patterns. Test thresholds adapt over time per workflow.

Why before-conclude, not after — testing AFTER means the operator sees a failure and has to ask CoS to redo. Testing BEFORE means CoS catches its own failures and retries silently. Accuracy KPI in §7 Observability measures: % of CoS outputs that passed first-time-test vs needed retry.

§4.6 HOW Methodology bridge (cross-reference)

Every Native primitive (§4.1) embeds a HOW choice from §3 HOW Methodology. Charters declare HOW preferences at creation; agents materialize with HOW context inherited; method routing (D.11) implements HOW-tier mapping; authority gate (D.6) defers to HOW's escalation logic. Native runtime is the executor; §3 HOW is the doctrine.

§4.7 Workforce outputs as deliverables

Native's workforce doesn't only produce text/decisions — it produces software + agentic systems that can become operator-owned artifacts. These outputs have three delivery destinations:

Delivery destinationWhat it isExampleOwnership
D1 · Shipped to clientNative builds a custom tool/dashboard/script for operator's company → delivered as that company's asset.Paisa Q3 SOM tracker dashboard built by Native, hosted in Paisa's own infra.Client (Paisa) owns; Native deletes its copy post-handoff.
D2 · Shipped back to Sutra-orgNative builds a tool/connector/skill that's reusable across operators → ratified into Sutra plugin or platform.Native builds a useful Slack bot pattern → Sutra adopts as a new connector for fleet use.Sutra-org owns; rolls into next plugin release per §6 Distribution lifecycle.
D3 · Operator-internal useNative builds a one-off script/agent for operator's own portfolio use across companies → operator keeps as internal tool.Operator's cross-portfolio analytics aggregator script built by Native, used per session.Operator owns; stays in operator's Native workspace.

Delivery pipeline owned by §6 Distribution. §4.7 declares the platform CAPABILITY (workforce can produce deliverable software/agents); §6 owns the delivery modes (how the artifact gets to its destination, packaging, lifecycle). All deliverables emit DecisionProvenance + Artifact Governance metadata (D.12): version, owner, source-Charter, audit trail.

§4.8 Capability Surface Map (CSM) — feature lifecycle visible to operators

Native + CoS ship features through an explicit lifecycle. CSM is the source-of-truth for what's stable vs proposed. Operators see CSM via sutra-capability skill on demand and via SessionStart banner.

StateWhat it meansOperator-visible signal
cap-NNN PROPOSEDCapability idea logged in CAPABILITY-MAP.md; design pendingShown as "proposed for next version" in capability digest; not invocable

DESIGNEDArchitecture + acceptance criteria ratified; implementation queued"Designed; building" tag with target version
BUILTImplementation merged; under verification (per §4.5 Test-before-conclude)Visible in plugin but flagged "Built; verifying"
SHIPPINGVerified; rolling out to fleet tiers per §6.0 (T1 → T2 → T3 → T4 cadence). Axis note (N38 resolution, 2026-06-12): this is the per-CAPABILITY rollout cadence — dogfood-first. It is a different axis from CLIENT-ONBOARDING order, which per D41 runs new-clients-first (publish → T0 Asawa → T4 new clients → T2/T3 deferred). Both hold; no contradiction.Operator gets release-note callout; CSM banner shows in active capabilities

Capability tiering across the fleet

  • L0 fleet-shipped — capability ships to all T2-T4 via plugin marketplace
  • L1 cohort-staged — capability live for selected cohort with promotion deadline (per §B.0 Build-Layer-style discipline)
  • L2 instance-local — capability stays in T1 Asawa only (governance / experimental)

Sources: holding/CAPABILITY-MAP.md · holding/state/capability-map-audit.jsonl · sutra-capability skill at sutra/marketplace/plugin/skills/sutra-capability/ · D34/D38 founder directions. A Module (per §1.4 Product Hierarchy) deploys via 3 artifacts. This is a conceptual contract first; filesystem layout below is one canonical implementation.

§4.9.2 Acceptance criteria (Module deployment v1)

  • SPEC update is operator-invisible (rolls in via plugin update per §6.4 Lifecycle).
  • BINDING is operator-editable (within §1.4.3 deployment authority phases) and Charter-scoped.
  • RUNTIME STATE per-tenant isolated; cross-tenant access default-denied (HS-2 fires on violation).
  • Module retire/decommission: SPEC pinned, BINDING archived, RUNTIME STATE preserved for replay window then trimmed per D.12 Artifact Governance.

§4.10 Resilience contract — operator-visible failure behavior

Native's hardstop catalog (HS-1..HS-8) defines what the system does when it cannot continue safely. The full engine spec lives at sutra/os/native/hardstops/HS-*.md; this section is the OPERATOR-VISIBLE summary — what each hardstop looks like in operator surface terms. The operator never debugs the system; the system tells the operator what happened, what's safe to do next, and what needs operator decision.

§4.10.1 Hardstop catalog (operator-visible behavior)

HardstopTrigger (engineering, full spec in canon)Operator-visible behavior
HS-1 reflexive-checkWorkflow attempted to modify Sutra core primitives without reflexive_check Constraint clearedSystem REFUSES to self-modify; surfaces "this change touches Sutra's own rules — please ratify directly" — operator can authorize or cancel
HS-2 governance-overheadPer-turn governance overhead exceeds 25% of token budgetSystem aborts the current turn; operator sees "turn aborted (governance overhead exceeded budget)"; next turn proceeds normally per canon
HS-3 tenant-boundaryExecution attempts cross-tenant access without explicit delegates_to edgeCross-company action DENIED per canon; operator gets a "this action would touch ; explicit delegates_to authorization required" prompt
HS-4 audit-unwritableDecisionProvenance log unwritable across all 3 fallback channelsSystem STOPS per canon; surfaces "cannot record this decision; halting until audit recovers" — no silent unaudited execution
HS-5 cutover-rollback-failsCutover rollback path itself fails to restore prior stateCutover PAUSED per canon; operator + Tenant owner brought into HITL (human-in-the-loop) for the decision; no automated retry
HS-6 governance-hooks-fail3+ distinct governance hooks fail within the same turnSESSION SUSPENDED per canon; resume requires manual investigation of the failing hooks before re-engagement
HS-7 codex-queue-staleCodex review queue exceeds 20 items OR any item more than 7 days staleShipment broadly BLOCKED per canon until queue drains; operator sees "review queue saturated — shipments paused"
HS-8 canary-regressionProduction canary observes regression against cutover behavior_invariantsAuto-ROLLBACK to prior version per canon; founder notified

Hardstop UX contract: every hardstop produces (a) a 1-2 sentence operator-facing message stating WHAT happened, (b) a recommended NEXT ACTION (decide / authorize / wait / investigate), (c) a link to the audit row for the event. Operator never has to read engineer-level stack traces; the system translates.

MECHANISM-PENDING (added 2026-05-13 per §10.10 row 1 · codex Lane A R12 P1): HS-4 · HS-5 · HS-6 · HS-7 · HS-8 are documented as canonical hardstops above, but the impl-vs-docs audit confirmed live detectors/executors do NOT yet exist in the shipped Native runtime — those rows are doctrine, mechanism pending. HS-1, HS-2, HS-3 have at least partial enforcement via existing plugin hooks. Resolution path per §10.10 P0 #1: either ship the missing detectors OR downgrade canon entries to "doctrine, mechanism pending" status. This callout is a place-holder hedge so the doc cannot self-contradict §10.10.

§4.10.2 Failure states (extension of §4.2 workforce state machine)

The state machine in §4.2 shows happy-path lifecycle (PROPOSED → ACTIVE → PERSISTED → RETIRED). Failure states extend this — these are the runtime states the workforce can enter when something doesn't go right:

Failure stateEntry conditionOperator visibilityExit path
PAUSEDHardstop fires (HS-N); OR Charter Authority requires explicit operator decision (per §D.6)Operator sees "task paused at step X — decision needed" with context + recommended next actionOperator decides → ACTIVE (resume) OR RETIRED (cancel)
ESCALATEDB3 retry budget exhausted (per §4.5 Test-before-conclude); OR Authority gate triggers ESCALATE class (per ADR-018 contract 1)Operator sees "auto-retry exhausted; your judgment needed" with B3a THINK 3-angle brainstorm + recommended angleOperator confirms direction → ACTIVE (B3b auto-executes the chosen path) OR RETIRED
DEGRADEDConnector half-fails (e.g., send succeeded but audit write missed); OR partial side-effect (draft created but send failed)Operator sees "step partially completed — message X sent but audit row missed; reconciliation needed" with compensation optionsReconciliation step (idempotent retry OR manual log-write) → ACTIVE (workflow continues) OR PAUSED (operator handles)

§4.10.3 §C.4 Risk → owning section map

Each R1-R9 in §C.4 Risks + mitigations maps to the section that owns its mitigation. Audit-clean: no risk is a one-line floating mitigation; every risk has a section answering "where is this controlled":

RiskOwner section
R1 Pattern-emergence false positives§2.B B5.d learned patterns (k=4 threshold) + §3 HOW selection
R2 Audit log unwritable§4.10 HS-4 + ADR-013 telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonl
R3 Cross-tenant leakage§4.10 HS-3 + ADR-006 tenant-isolation-domain-field
R4 Operator review fatigue§D.6 Authority Model trust signal + ADR-018 agentic-systems-pattern contract 1 (AUTO/GATE/ESCALATE classification)
R5 Pricing model wrong§C.1 v1 → v2 pricing experiment + §6.2 Pricing + packaging
R6 v1 wedge too narrow§A.5 Differentiation + §A.6 Scope — wedge validates personalization + governance + audit before TAM expansion (v2)
R7 Person-formation overfits§2.B B5.d long baseline window + operator opt-in ratings (per §A.6 C7 constraint)
R8 Multi-agent cost spikes§3 HOW cascade dimension budget + ADR-018 contract 3 (per-Charter complexity budget)
R9 Canon driftD54 canon-only memory + §4.5 Test-before-conclude + PROTO-019 codex + deepseek per-change review

§4.12 Sutra→Native Parity — frozen Sutra/Asawa concept universe tracked against Native PRD coverage

FRAMEWORK LOCKED 2026-06-11 (founder) · ROWS SHIPPED 2026-06-12 — T1-T8 COMPLETE. Founder direction: Native must capture every Sutra/Asawa concept present in the frozen 2026-06-11 source set below; anything in that bounded set that is not captured becomes a requirement here. This section holds only the parity framework (universe · fit model · statuses · schema · scoreboard); it does not redefine Native scope, reopen §1.0 schema, replace canon routing, or assert per-concept status before T3 verification. The per-concept ledger rows landed 2026-06-12 after extraction + adversarial verification (plan tasks T1-T3); see the §4.12.5 scoreboard. Plan of record: holding/plans/sutra-native-parity-v1.0/PLAN.md (dual-lane COMPLETE 2026-06-11: deepseek design-review MODIFY-7 absorbed; codex retro MODIFY-6 absorbed). Every gate of this project is dual-lane reviewed: codex AND deepseek, both verdicts required.

§4.12.2 Fit model — three altitudes relative to the Native product (LOCKED)

Every Sutra/Asawa concept sits at exactly one primary altitude relative to this product:

AltitudeWhat it isSutra/Asawa familiesNative home
UPSTREAM — governs the productInvariants the product must obeyFounding Doctrine · D1-D55 · charters · Asawa L0 constructs · Hierarchy L0-L5 (Asawa principles P1-P10 ride at L1 of that family)Authority + Tenancy wrap · §9 Meta (D.6 Authority Model, D.13 Founder Directions) · canon pillars/ bucket
CORE — is the productMechanisms the runtime executesEngines · active PROTOs · per-turn blocks · gate mechanismsDaemon (Orchestration + SoP) · Host · §4 runtime · ADRs
DOWNSTREAM — distributes/observes the productHow the product reaches operators + reports backPlugin packaging · skills surface · CSM lifecycle · fleet analytics/telemetryUI · §6 Distribution · §7 Observability · SoR

Projection rule: altitude is assigned per CONCEPT, not per artifact. A concept may project onto a second altitude (e.g. BLUEPRINT: core mechanism + downstream skill surface) — the ledger stores primary altitude + a projection note. The §1.0 schema stays LOCKED — parity may add ledger rows and routed requirements only; it does not block §1.0 or create new architecture blocks without ADR approval. Closed 2026-06-12: T2 assigned altitudes; founder ratified exclusions at T2.5 under standing delegation (retro-overridable).

§4.12.3 Statuses + ledger row schema (LOCKED)

StatusMeaningConsequence
CAPTUREDVerbatim or equivalent exists in this PRD/canon — anchor citedNone — row closed
PARTIALNamed but under-specified — gap_description says exactly what's thinRequirement authored for the gap
MISSINGNo trace in PRD or canon — survived an adversarial refute-passRequirement authored (Needs N25+ and/or canon part-file/ADR per D54 routing)
EXCLUDEDDeliberately not Native scope — reason mandatory (e.g. asawa-only caps)Founder ratifies each at the T2.5 gate; reversible

Row schema: concept_id · name · family · source_path · altitude(U/C/D) · native_home(block+§) · status · evidence_anchor · gap_description · requirement_ref · excluded_reason. Namespace: family/label — e.g. cap/001, proto/021, dir/D38, engine/blueprint, doctrine/p0. Ledger of record: holding/SUTRA-NATIVE-PARITY.md + append-only holding/state/sutra-native-parity-audit.jsonl (CSM precedent — §4.8 pattern); this section renders the scoreboard + framework only. Closed 2026-06-12: T4 materialized the ledger; T5 routed requirements (Needs N25-N41 + dated backlog); ADR-021 accepted via D54.

§4.12.4 Placement decision — Sutra plugin host residency (ADR-021 candidate)

Closes Host block open gap #4 ("where does the Sutra plugin live in the block diagram"): the Sutra plugin is HOST-RESIDENT — the governance layer the Host loads per-session. Hooks = Host lifecycle interceptors · skills = Host-invokable bundles · per-turn blocks = Host-emitted discipline. Daemon-side analogs (workflow registry, EngineEvents) live in Orchestration/SoP. Upstream governance content the plugin carries (defaults, directions) is Authority+Tenancy material transported by the Host-resident layer. No new block needed. Status: ACCEPTED 2026-06-12 — sutra/os/decisions/ADR-021-sutra-plugin-host-residency.md (D54 routing); Host block open gap #4 CLOSED. Dual-lane: deepseek reviewed this run; codex retro queued (auth).

§5 Connector — transport layer #

PurposeMove messages + tool calls between Native (§4) and the world outside (humans, services, peer Sutras).
OwnsSlack · email · web · browser · phone · 3rd-party tool integrations · inbound/outbound message handling · retry semantics.
Depends onD.6 Authority gate (at every send); §7 Observability (for delivery health).
Does NOT ownWhat to say (B3); decision logic (B3); Charter scope (§4); audience selection (B4 + Authority).

Email (Resend)LiveoutDaily pulse + notifications
Web (fetch/search)LiveoutFor B2.b world's-best context
Browser (co-work)Charter TODO-005in + outDeferred to v1.x; spec pending
Phone (voice + SMS)Not planned v1—v2+
3rd-party toolsPluggable via Composio (Path B long-term)outPer-tool Charter scoping
Peer-Native A2Av1 log-only stub—Full transport v2+ (B2.c · B4 peer)

§5.2 Inbound / outbound flow

                   §4 Native Platform
                          │
              ┌───────────┴───────────┐
              │                       │
   outbound   ▼                       ▲   inbound
   (B3 → B4)  │                       │   (B4 → B3)
              │                       │
   ┌──────────┴──────┐       ┌────────┴─────────┐
   │  §5 CONNECTOR   │       │  §5 CONNECTOR    │
   │  ─────────────  │       │  ─────────────   │
   │  Authority gate │       │  Identity verify │
   │  draft → typed  │       │  payload → typed │
   │  envelope       │       │  envelope        │
   │  retry logic    │       │  route to B3     │
   └────────┬────────┘       └────────▲─────────┘
            │                          │
            ▼                          │
   ┌───────────────────────────────────┴────────┐
   │  THE WORLD (Slack · email · web · 3rd-pty) │
   └────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   Every send: Authority-gated · DecisionProvenance row · retry on transient fail.
   Every receive: Identity-verified · typed-envelope · routed to B3 next action.

§5.3 Acceptance criteria (Connector v1)

  • Every outbound call passes through Authority gate; default-deny on missing scope.
  • Inbound from unverified identity logs + drops, doesn't enter B3.
  • Transient failures retry; permanent failures surface to operator (no silent drop).
  • Per-tool scoping enforced via Charter (e.g. "Slack:#paisa-team only" not "Slack:*").

§6 Distribution — go-to-market #

PurposeHow CoS reaches operators. Business-layer concerns: channel, pricing, onboarding, lifecycle.
OwnsChannels (Sutra plugin · direct install · cloud) · pricing tiers · onboarding flow · update/upgrade lifecycle · feedback loop.
Depends on§2 CoS product readiness; §4 Native platform stability; §7 Observability (acquisition + activation metrics).
Does NOT ownProduct surface; runtime behavior; transport.

§6.0 Asawa Client Taxonomy — T1-T4 distribution tiers

Distribution depends on which tier of client Native is serving. Per founder direction (D34, 2026-04-24), 4 tiers exist with uniform governance but different identity / ownership / onboarding paths.

TierNameWhoOnboarding pathPlugin distribution
T1Asawa (the holding co)Sankalp Asawa, founder · CEO of Asawa Inc.Direct — Asawa runs Native on its own infrastructure; full authority accessPlugin embedded; auto-update
T2Owned portfolioAsawa-owned products: DayFlow · Billu · Paisa · PPR · MazeSutra-onboard 8-phase playbook; founder is principal at eachPlugin auto-update; pinned per company
T3Projects (client-owned IP)Asawa does the work but client owns IP: Testlify · DharmikSutra-onboard playbook adapted; client retains commercial product; Asawa runs deliveryPlugin distributed; client may pin to specific version
T4Sutra Users (external)Operators in the wider market who adopt the Sutra plugin themselvesSelf-onboard via Sutra plugin; tracked via holding/departments/analytics/fleet.shPlugin marketplace; opt-in updates

Why this matters for Native: each tier has different governance scope (T1 full authority · T4 plugin-policy-only). Charter scope + Authority Model thresholds (D.6) default differently per tier. Source: holding/ASAWA-AS-CLIENT.md · CLAUDE.md identity section · founder direction D34.

ChannelAudienceStatus v1
Sutra plugin (T2 + T4 fleet)Existing Sutra users / Asawa portfolioPrimary v1 channel
Direct install (terminal-fluent)Operator-types with own setupv1
Cloud (hosted)Operator-types who don't want infrav2+
Partner-deployed (e.g. accelerators)Operators in cohort programsv2+

§6.2 Pricing + packaging (placeholder v1)

  • Tier A — Solo founder (1-2 companies)
  • Tier B — Portfolio operator (3-7 companies)
  • Tier C — Enterprise / partner-deployed
  • Pricing TBD; placeholder pending operator-economics data.

§6.3 Onboarding flow

Per the Sutra-onboard 8-phase playbook (intake → market → shape → decide → architect → configure → deploy → activate). New CoS operator goes through:

   STAGE 1  Install (Sutra plugin / direct)
        │
        ▼
   STAGE 2  Charter setup (operator declares fixed Charters
            for each company in portfolio)
        │
        ▼
   STAGE 3  Connect (Slack · email · tools per Charter)
        │
        ▼
   STAGE 4  First-turn — operator types intent; CoS responds;
            Authority gate dialed conservative by default
        │
        ▼
   STAGE 5  Ongoing — operator dials autonomy + feeds patterns;
            B5.d learns; CoS becomes faster per company

§6.3.1 Install + onboard responsibility per tier

Per §6.0 Asawa Client Taxonomy, install/onboard responsibility differs per tier. The §A.2.2 zero-terminal post-onboarding rule means SOMEONE handles the one-time technical setup; this matrix names who, per tier.

TierWho installs Native + CoSOnboarding channelOperator terminal exposure
T1 Asawa Inc.Asawa Forge self-installs (we are the platform)Internal — Forge engineers + founderFounder is terminal-fluent; uses terminal for governance + canon work
T2 Owned (DayFlow · Billu · Paisa · PPR · Maze)Asawa/Sutra Forge installs + maintainsForge-led — Forge engineers set up per company; Charter created with company-specific contextNone post-onboarding — operator (T2 founder) uses CoS surface only
T3 Projects (Testlify · Dharmik)Asawa Forge installs into client environment; client owns the deployment + IPForge-led intake → Sutra-onboard 8-phase playbook → handoff to client operatorNone post-handoff — client operator uses CoS surface only
T4 Fleet (external Sutra plugin adopters)Operator self-installs via Sutra plugin marketplace (claude plugin install sutra)Plugin auto-onboarding — /core:start command guides setupOne-time terminal use for plugin install; post-install CoS surface in their Claude Code session (zero terminal beyond install)

Tier transition path: T4 adopters who want managed install (less terminal exposure) can graduate to T3 (Forge-led install) on request. T3 clients who internalize the platform can stay T3 or migrate to T2-owned with operator-led ongoing. Per §A.2.2: regardless of tier, the operator never sees terminal commands during ongoing operation — the matrix above names who handles the one-time setup that the constraint factors out.

§6.4 Lifecycle + updates

  • Sutra plugin auto-update path; CoS-product updates ride the plugin channel for T2/T4.
  • Operator can pin a CoS version per company (Charter-scoped).
  • Breaking changes flagged in operator-facing changelog before push.

§6.5 Transformation Cycle — customer onboarding journey beyond install

New CoS operators come from existing organizational contexts. The transformation cycle is the structured path from "I have an existing workflow" to "CoS-on-Native runs my workflow." Workshops + sessions + co-working — not just install.

§6.5.1 Five-stage transformation cycle

   ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐

§6.5.3 Acceptance criteria (Transformation Cycle v1)

  • Discovery → Articulation in ≤1 week per operator.
  • Pilot tasks have explicit success/fail criteria (per §4.5 Test-before-conclude).
  • Steady-state target: operator confidence + CoS accuracy KPI both ≥80% by end of Expansion stage.
  • Quarterly review surfaces drift signals from §7 Observability; re-onboard triggered if drift >15%.

§6.7 Module distribution + lifecycle

How Modules (per §1.4 Product Hierarchy) reach operators. §6.0-§6.6 cover Product-level distribution; §6.7 covers Module-level distribution within an operator's existing CoS install.

§6.7.1 Module lifecycle (cradle to retire)

StageWhat happensAuthority
ProposedCapability already exists in CSM (cap-NNN); composing it into a Module candidateAsawa Forge proposes
DesignedModule SPEC drafted; binding schema declared; codex+deepseek review (per §4.5 test gate)Asawa Forge designs; Sutra plugin core approves
Built + ShippedSPEC merged to plugin and PUBLISHED to the catalog; installs receive an "update available" signal — the update lands when the OPERATOR pulls it (PULL-default per founder doctrine D22, resolved N33 2026-06-12). PUSH (forced auto-roll) is reserved for security/foundational fixes only, and announces itselfNative platform publishes; operator pulls (push = security exception)
Operator-installedOperator chooses Module from approved catalog (v1.5+); creates BINDING for a CharterOperator (per §1.4.3 phase)
ActiveRUNTIME STATE accumulates; observability metrics tracked; drift detectedNative runtime
DeprecatedModule marked end-of-life in CSM; sunset window announced; operators notified via feedback channelNative platform + operator-affecting notification
RetiredSPEC archived; existing BINDINGs frozen-readable; RUNTIME STATE preserved for replay window per D.12Native platform

Update policy (N33 resolution, 2026-06-12): PULL is the default for every behavior-affecting Module update — operators choose when their company's setup changes (doctrine D22). PUSH is the override, reserved for security and foundational fixes, always operator-visible (release-note callout + audit row). This matches how the Sutra plugin behaves today (/core:update is a manual pull).

§6.7.2 v1 distribution scope (per R7 reviewer convergence)

  • v1 — Native platform + Asawa Forge author + ship Modules; operators consume passively (bound by Forge for them)
  • v1.5 — operator self-serve binding from approved Module catalog (still no author-new-Module privilege)
  • v2+ — third-party developers gated by review + permissioning + audit (marketplace concern)

§6.7.3 Acceptance criteria (Module distribution v1)

  • Module catalog is operator-discoverable via sutra-capability skill (single source of truth: §4.8 CSM).
  • Module retirement preserves replay capability for operator-held BINDINGs (per D.12).
  • Cross-Product Module reuse is designed for, not guaranteed in v1 — interfaces stay clean enough to extract later when 2nd Product exists.

§7 Observability — how we know it works #

PurposeInstrument every layer so operator + Asawa team can see whether CoS is doing what it promises, and where it's drifting.
OwnsPer-pillar quality metrics · drift signals · operator satisfaction signals · audit + replay surfaces.
Depends onAll layers (instruments cross-cuttingly).
Does NOT ownDecision execution; transport; pricing.

§7.1 Per-pillar metrics

PillarPrimary metricDrift signal
B1 Ask my wayIntent parse accuracy (intent_type + sub-intents match operator's correction frequency)Correction rate >15% per week → drift
B2 World's best + mineContext relevance (operator drops <10% of retrieved context)Drop rate >25% → drift
B3 Think + Do + PresentAuto-vs-ask ratio; outcome quality (operator accepts >80% of B3b outputs)Accept rate falls >15pp WoW
B4 Talk for meOutbound success rate; audience-calibration accuracyOperator overrides voice >20% of sends
B5 RememberReplay latency (≤30s/day-window v1); cross-tenant default-deny verifiedReplay latency >60s OR any cross-tenant leak

§7.1.A Accuracy KPI (integration with §4.5 Test-before-conclude)

MetricDefinitionTarget v1Drift signal
First-time-test pass rate% of CoS outputs that pass the pre-declared §4.5 test on first try (no retry needed)≥85% T0, ≥75% T1, ≥65% T2, ≥55% T3 Charter milestonesDrop >10pp WoW per task tier
Retry-to-conclude ratioAvg retries per concluded task (test-fail → B3 retry loop iterations)≤1.3 T0, ≤2.0 T1, ≤3.0 T2/T3Ratio rises >0.5 WoW
Escalation rate (test-fail-exhausted)% of tasks where retry budget exhausted → operator escalation≤5% across all tiers>10% any tier → workflow review
Post-conclude operator-correction rate% of CoS-concluded outputs operator subsequently corrects (test missed the failure)≤8% T0/T1, ≤15% T2/T3Rate rises → test criteria need tightening

Accuracy is a per-workflow KPI, not a single number. Each task tier (T0-T3) + each pillar carries its own accuracy band. §4.5 Test-before-conclude is where the test runs; §7.1.A is where the aggregated results live.

§7.2 Audit + replay surfaces

The audit promise of B5.c made visible. Every Native turn produces 5 artifacts; observability surfaces all 5 to operator on demand.

   PER TURN AUDIT TRAIL (operator can replay any of these)

   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  1. INTENT RECORD       (B1)                     │
   │     parsed intent + sub-intents + schema preview │
   └────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┘
                            ▼
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  2. CONTEXT MANIFEST    (B2)                     │
   │     ranked artifact list used; what was dropped  │
   └────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┘
                            ▼
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  3. DECISION PROVENANCE (B3)                     │
   │     phases + factors + authority verdict + output│
   └────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┘
                            ▼
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  4. OUTBOUND LOG        (B4, if comms triggered) │
   │     audience + channel + draft + sent + replies  │
   └────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┘
                            ▼
   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │  5. SUBSTRATE WRITE     (B5)                     │
   │     active-session ref + learned-pattern updates │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   Operator command: "replay last Paisa turn" → all 5 artifacts shown chronologically.
   Operator command: "show me what changed in B5.d this week" → diff of learned patterns.

§7.3 Operator satisfaction signals

  • Time-to-decision (operator decides on CoS output in <30s — CX bar gate)
  • Refusal-to-execute rate (operator says "don't do this, ask me first")
  • Autonomy-dial direction (does operator dial autonomy up or down per domain over time?)
  • Surface-switching frequency (operator should NOT switch surfaces mid-task per CX bar)

§7.4 Acceptance criteria (Observability v1)

  • 5-artifact audit trail emitted per turn (HS-4 fires on any missing channel).
  • Operator can request replay for any past turn within ≤30s for a single-day window.
  • Drift signals tracked weekly; breach surfaces to founder via Daily Pulse.

§7.5 Daily Pulse — operator's daily aggregate dashboard (Module per §1.4)

Daily Pulse is the visible artifact of §2.E S5 whole-system state. Auto-generated by holding/hooks/observability-synthesis.sh from session-end data. Operator reads once/day to see what CoS did + drift signals across the portfolio.

Hierarchy tag: Daily Pulse is a Module per §1.4 Product Hierarchy — composes cap-Telemetry + cap-Audit + cap-CrossPortfolio-Priority. Independent lifecycle. Per-Deployment BINDING (operator config: pulse cadence, channel, signal thresholds). Own RUNTIME STATE (daily-pulse archive per tenant). Reusable across future products on Native (designed for, not guaranteed v1).

§7.5.1 What Daily Pulse contains

SectionContent
Per-Charter statusEach company's Charter — active tasks · completed today · pending operator decisions · drift signals
Cross-portfolio priorityWhat matters now across all N companies — top 3-5 priorities surfaced via §3 HOW + §6 importance ranking
Accuracy snapshotFirst-time-test pass rate across task tiers (per §7.1.A); week-over-week trend
Workforce healthCharter status (active · drifting · stale); agent materializations + outcomes
Feedback summaryNew operator feedback artifacts since last pulse; product team triage status
Token / RTK efficiencyToken usage trend; RTK gain measurement; cost-per-task aggregate
Outstanding gatesAuthority-gated actions pending operator approval (per D.6)

§7.5.2 Operator commands

  • /asawa — CEO dashboard for portfolio overview
  • /status — current session status + drift signals
  • Daily Pulse delivered via holding/OBSERVABILITY-PULSE.md + optional email/Slack push per Charter

§7.5.3 Acceptance criteria (Daily Pulse v1)

  • Pulse auto-generates within 60s of session-end (no manual trigger needed).
  • Operator scans pulse + decides next actions in ≤2 minutes (CX bar gate per D.7).
  • Drift breach surfaced visually (boxed callout) — impossible to miss.
  • Cross-portfolio priorities ranked by Charter weight + recency + risk per §3 HOW decomposition.

Sources: holding/OBSERVABILITY-PULSE.md · holding/ANALYTICS-PULSE.md · holding/hooks/observability-synthesis.sh · holding/scripts/daily-review.sh (departments/observability) · founder direction "cadence is hours not days".

C.6 Open questions which we need to tackle in the future #

Distinct from §C.3 Open questions (v1 design questions; all ratified 2026-05-09). These are forward-looking open questions about ongoing Native capability — surfaced 2026-05-13 by founder; not yet ratified. Each may produce ADRs in canon (engineering decisions) + PRD body updates (operator-facing contracts).

#Open questionWhere it touches existing PRD
FQ1HOW methodology — open questions about Native's HOW Methodology engine. Which approach for which situation? When does cascading recursion stop? How are HOW choices learned vs. operator-overridden? When are HOW choices made silently vs. surfaced for ratification?Foundation in §3 HOW Methodology (6 HOW dimensions); operator interaction in §2.B B3a THINK; learning loop in §2.B B5.d learned patterns
FQ2Accuracy — open questions about Native's accuracy contract. What KPIs measure accuracy per task tier (T0-T3)? What's the right test depth at each phase? When does test-before-conclude become test-by-sample (cost tradeoff)? How does accuracy compound vs degrade over time?Foundation in §4.5 Test-before-conclude gate; observability in §7 Observability; failure surfacing in HS-1 / HS-4 hardstops
FQ3Change-impact awareness for existing systems (including 2nd/3rd-order effects) — when making a change to an operator's existing system, how does Native ensure it (a) identifies ALL relevant things that might be affected by the change FIRST, (b) considers all relevant things that need to be considered for making the change, and (c) understands second-order and third-order effects of making the change? What is the cascading-impact-analysis engine — and how does the operator inspect what was checked vs missed?Touches: Authority Model (§D.6) escalation triggers; HOW Cascading dimension (§3.1); Workforce state machine (§4.2); B3a THINK mode for novel-state work. Gap: no current PRD section formally specifies cascade-impact analysis as a Native capability. T-G4-07 T-G4-08 T-G4-09

Status: PROPOSED 2026-05-13. Not yet ratified. Founder surfaced these forward; they enter a future research + design cycle (likely codex + deepseek consult round per question, producing ADRs in canon + capability contracts in PRD body).

G3Pattern emergence proposes high-value automationsProposal approve rate≥50% rolling 30dNative (Pattern detection)

D.6 Authority Model (cross-cutting) #

Six dimensions gate Native's auto-execute vs ask-operator decisions. Applies cross-pillar — most heavily in B3 (action gate) and B4 (delegation through Native to humans).

DimensionWhat it gates
by domainNative acts in one company's domain but not another (per Charter)
by toolNative can call Slack but not bank-API without auth
by stakeholderNative emails team but escalates board/regulator
by dollar threshold≤$X auto; >$X ask
by reversibilityreversible auto; irreversible ask
by confidence levelhigh-confidence auto; low-confidence ask

D.7 CX Bar (cross-cutting) #

Quality bar Native's experience must clear — applies to every pillar.

  • Feels like a good Chief of Staff, not a robot
  • Scannable output; operator decides in <30s of reading
  • Low friction; routine work doesn't interrupt
  • Brainstorm partnership feels collaborative, not deferential
  • Errors surface clearly with retry, never silently

D.8 Operator-First Lens (cross-cutting) #

Native's loyalty is to the operator personally; org is the mission frame, not the master. Shapes context ranking, escalation, memory formation in every pillar.

  • B2 ranks context by what serves the operator's decision
  • B3 escalation defaults favor operator's pace, not org SLAs
  • B5 memory is per-operator + per-Charter; org-Tenant artifacts visible by ACL
  • Conflict between operator preference and org policy: surface to operator, never auto-resolve to org

D.9 Scope anti-pattern (cross-cutting) #

Native v1 is NOT — explicit scope guardrails.

  • NOT a day manager (no calendar / todos)
  • NOT a chatbot (workflow-shaped, not conversational)
  • NOT a memory product (governance + multi-tenant + emergence are the value)
  • NOT a full-autonomy agent (operator-in-loop is core)
  • NOT a calendar / inbox / task-list (substantive cognitive work only)

D.10 Charter Model (Native platform primitive · cross-cutting) #

Charter is a Native platform primitive — an operator-set behavioral contract that scopes a deployed workforce instance (e.g., the CoS product) over time. Each Charter is either fixed (rules locked) or flexible (rules adapt with operator approval).

Charter modeWhat it meansExample
FixedScope + decisions + authority + tools all locked at Charter creation; Native cannot expand without operator re-charter"Run weekly P&L close — only these tools, only this audience, escalate on deltas >$X"
FlexibleScope + tools may extend within declared bounds; Native proposes, operator ratifies; learned patterns become standing rules"Run BillU GTM brainstorm — adapt method based on what works, propose new tools as needed, escalate before external comms"

Charter mode interacts with D.6 Authority Model: fixed Charters set conservative authority defaults; flexible Charters allow authority to widen with operator approvals. Anchored to T3 Charter tier in A.7.

D.11 Method Selection (Native platform primitive · cross-cutting) #

Method Selection is a Native platform primitive. Different work classes need different intelligence — Native routes per task tier (A.7) to the right model, tool set, and decision pattern. CoS (the deployed product) consumes this routing without re-implementing it.

Task tierOptimization targetMethod profile
T0 AtomicSpeed; minimize tokensFast model · single LLM call · narrow tool set · no escalation
T1 Multi-actionReliability across N stepsStandard model · per-step verify · tool-routing · gate on external comms
T2 IterativeResponsiveness; low-latency refinementFast model · short context window · 1-min iteration loop · zero gate on minor edits
T3 CharterLong-horizon quality + judgmentBest-available model · max context · multi-method ensemble · gates at goal-transition points

Method selection is Ring 2 contract material (what Native commits to per task tier). Method routing engine internals live in Phase 2 design — see action-engine-design.md.

D.12 Artifact Governance (cross-cutting) #

Combined Artifact Discipline (form) + Artifact Integrity (lifecycle). Every artifact Native produces must satisfy both surfaces. Per codex+deepseek R5 convergence: separating these two invites duplicate rules + drift.

D.12.1 Form discipline — transform text prose → technical-specific documents

RuleWhat it means
Right artifact shape per taskPlans → checklist · designs → ASCII diagram + table · decisions → ADR · code outputs → versioned source files · data → typed schema. Default text only when nothing else fits.
Visual + structured + wholeFor ≥3 items: table > prose · for decisions: ASCII-boxed callout · for scores: progress bar · for flows: ASCII diagram. Double presentation (visual + text) acceptable.
LLM-future-readableASCII diagrams over images (machine-readable). Mermaid-style text diagrams acceptable. Avoid screenshots unless source-of-truth.
Scientific appropriatenessUse the format that the discipline uses (Gantt for time-plans, swim-lane for processes, dependency-graph for systems, ER for data, sequence for protocols).

D.12.2 Lifecycle integrity — append-not-overwrite

RuleWhat it means
Append, don't overwriteEvery change adds a version (semver or date-stamped); the prior version stays accessible. No silent rewrites.
Notify-on-changeWhen an artifact shared with audience X changes, audience X is notified by §5 Connector (Slack / email per Charter).
Reference-with-versionEvery cross-reference embeds the version it points to (e.g., paisa-Q3-onepager@v3 not paisa-Q3-onepager). Resolves to that exact version even after newer versions ship.
Pristine boundariesArtifact identity stays stable. Metadata (owner, version, source-of-truth status) travels with the artifact, not in a sidecar that can desync.
Audit-traceableEvery artifact write produces a DecisionProvenance row (§4 Native audit substrate) linking creator-Charter + reason + diff-from-prior.

D.12.3 Acceptance criteria (Artifact Governance v1)

  • 100% of Native-produced artifacts carry version + owner + Charter-ref metadata.
  • Zero silent overwrites (HS-4 fires on any write that replaces without version-bump).
  • Notify-on-change reaches audience within 60s of change for Slack/email channels.
  • Cross-references resolve to versioned target; broken references surface to operator (don't silently 404).

D.13 Founder Directions framework (cross-cutting) #

Append-only log of governance directions (D1-D55+) that shape Native's behavior. Every direction has a TRIGGER (when it fires) + CHECK (how compliance is verified) + ENFORCEMENT (hook or convention). Direction-driven governance is itself a Native product feature, not Asawa-internal-only.

D.13.1 Anatomy of a Founder Direction

FieldContent
IDD{N} — append-only integer; never re-used
TriggerWhen this direction activates (utterance pattern · file pattern · session-start · etc.)
CheckHow compliance is verified (hook · skill · convention)
EnforcementSOFT (advisory) · HARD (blocks tool call) · CONVENTION (relies on Claude discipline)
Revoke phraseHow operator can disable mid-session (e.g., "stop caveman", "ask before push")
OwnerDirection-DRI (Direction Responsible Individual) — usually CEO of Asawa for D-family

D.13.2 Direction-driven product behavior — example slice

DirectionWhat CoS does differentlyEnforcement
D34 Asawa Client TaxonomyOnboarding flow + Authority defaults vary per tier (see §6.0)CONVENTION
D38 Plugin-first build-layerAsawa-T2 work defaults to plugin L0 (per §4.8 CSM)HARD via build-layer-check.sh
D48 BLUEPRINT Output + Verified-by fieldsFoundational edits require Output + Verified-by per §4.5 Test-before-concludeHARD via blueprint-check.sh
D51 Caveman defaultProse terse for Asawa/Sutra work; code/governance blocks stay normalCONVENTION via skill
D52 Autonomous git pushRoutine push without per-push gate; destructive ops still gatedCONVENTION via session policy
D53 Context7 defaultLibrary/SDK/framework Q always routes through Context7 MCPCONVENTION via skill
D54 Native canon-only routingNative sole source = NATIVE-ENGINE.md + ADR-004..017HARD via native-canon hook
D55 Structure-FirstAdding new thing fires Survey→Reorg→Simplify→SurfaceSOFT via structure-first-reminder.sh

D.13.3 Why directions ARE a Native product feature

  • Operators see direction impact in every CoS turn (Caveman prose · autonomous push · skill-explain card · Authority gate posture).
  • Directions evolve the system without code changes — D-family is the operator's leverage on platform behavior.
  • Direction history is auditable — append-only log at holding/FOUNDER-DIRECTIONS.md; every D{N} dated + sourced.
  • Direction registry is operator-readable — Daily Pulse references active directions; CSM (§4.8) tracks which directions affect which capabilities.

D.13.4 Acceptance criteria (Founder Directions v1)

  • Every direction has TRIGGER/CHECK/ENFORCEMENT/OWNER fields filled.
  • Directions are append-only; never re-numbered or silently revised (per D.12 Artifact Governance).
  • Operator can revoke any session-level direction via documented phrase (e.g., "stop caveman").
  • HARD-enforced directions ship with hook + override path ({D_TAG}_ACK=1 {D_TAG}_ACK_REASON='...').

Sources: holding/FOUNDER-DIRECTIONS.md (D1-D55+ append-only log) · holding/DIRECTION-ENFORCEMENT.md (hook coverage table) · holding/DIRECTIONS-AUDIT-2026-04-22.md (compliance audit).

Eight rules. Apply when adding to or editing this PRD, and when authoring future Layer-B Product PRDs per ADR-020. Each rule traces to a recorded source (commit · directive · ADR · memory).

#RuleSource
1Cagan — PRD body = WHAT the operator observes. Engine HOW lives in sutra/os/native/** + ADRs, not in this PRD.memory feedback_prd_what_not_how.md
2Operator voice — Ring 1 (Problem · Need · Solution direction) written first-person in the founder's voice. Match the §0 Tuesday narratives register. Not Cagan-formatted (that's Ring 2's job).directive 1778586433 §R4 #5

Practical use: before every PRD edit, scan rules 1-3 (voice + scope discipline). Before every multi-line addition, scan rules 4-7 (markers + sections + render + preservation). Before every merge, scan rule 8 (ratification). Authoring drift is caught at D.3 Review methodology 12-item checklist.

§10.1 Operating System Primitives & Unit Theory

Rationale: Native is an operating system, not an application. Every requirement in §1-§4 rests on a small set of OS primitives (Unit · Kernel · Hook · Registry · Effector · Signal · Cadence) plus the Unit theory (bounded work with A/E/K substrate + C coordination + M authority + 8 lifecycle phases + governance envelope + Cynefin gate + 9 informational dimensions + 7 VSM-mapped roles). Get these wrong → foundation rebuild. Get them right → product features become thin shells over proven primitives.

ADR-001 through ADR-020 ratify the decision-provenance system, approval-gate primitive, and organic-emergence propose/approve pattern.

§10.3 Holding Company Operating Model

Rationale: Sutra is not a product FOR companies — Asawa is a company that CONTAINS other companies. Holding-company research concluded Asawa is closer to LVMH/Tata than Berkshire/Alphabet: centralized shared infrastructure + decentralized brand autonomy + portfolio-level capital allocation. Exactly 3 functions must be owned at holding level: Governance Office · Portfolio Office · Shared Infrastructure. Everything else is Unit-scoped.

FindingSource
Asawa Inc. = programmable holding company; 3 novelties: culture-is-code · governance-automated · infrastructure-is-the-moatsutra/layer1-abstraction/RESEARCH-SYNTHESIS.md

Asawa owns 3 functions: Governance Office · Portfolio Office · Shared Infrastructure (everything else Unit-scoped)holding/research/HOLDING-COMPANY-RESEARCH.md
LVMH/Tata model closest fit — not Berkshire (because portfolio companies SHARE OS/hooks/protocols, not just capital)same
Universal holdco pattern: capital allocation · CEO selection · brand/standards · shared services · strategic directionsame
Cross-functional ops research (Amazon · Apple · Stripe · Spotify): converge on written coordination artifacts + explicit ownership + constrained focus + interface-based collaboration; steal mechanisms not mythologysutra/layer1-abstraction/CROSS-FUNCTIONAL-OPS-RESEARCH.md
D34 4-tier client taxonomy: T1 Asawa · T2 Owned (DayFlow/Billu/Paisa/PPR/Maze) · T3 Projects (Testlify/Dharmik) · T4 Sutra Users (external fleet)memory/project_asawa_client_taxonomy.md · D34

§10.5 Identity, Tenant & Capability Boundaries

Rationale: A multi-tenant programmable holding company cannot function without hard boundaries. Tenant isolation as a first-class field on Domain (ADR-006) + 4-tier client taxonomy (D34) + PERMISSIONS Tier 1/2/3 + identity-on-wire opt-in (D50) + caveman default (D51) collectively define where one entity ends and another begins.

§10.7 Founder Operating Doctrine (D1-D55)

Rationale: 55 ratified Founder Directions form a behavioral ABI. D1-D37 are general operating doctrine (how Sutra thinks, decides, prioritizes); D38-D55 are Native + fleet-specific directives (rollout order, capability boundaries, operator interaction, canon discipline). Per D6 — directions are executable with regression tests; per D55 — every direction triggers Survey → Reorg → Simplify → Surface on add.

§10.7.A General Operating Doctrine — selected entries from D1-D37

This sub-section enumerates a selected slice of D1-D37 (not the full set). For the complete + authoritative list with audit trail, see holding/FOUNDER-DIRECTIONS.md.

D#Direction
D1Simplicity through human confidence — few decisive signals over exhaustive detail
D2Thoroughness scales with the change — lifecycle constant, depth varies
D3Every task has a Sutra path — absorb new path, never bypass
D4Model directions as living forces — model downstream consequences
D5External research at every level
D6Directions are executable with regression tests
D7Expand then contract — growth → simplification cycles
D8Output-driven metrics everywhere
D9Recursive estimation engine (estimate-before · measure-after · feedback-compress)
D11Fix the process, not just the instance
D13Cascade downstream immediately
D14Don't stop — default to continued execution
D1570/20/10 products first
D17Don't miss parallel statements — capture multi-statement turns completely
D21Speed of building is a factor
D24Parallelization while evolving
D26Gear = decomposition granularity
D27Sutra→company deploys run at Depth 5
D28Per-turn routing and depth enforcement
D29Sutra plugin is Sutra; Asawa hidden in marketplace
D30a/bShip is not done — operationalize every build; policy ships with mechanism
D33External clients firewalled from Asawa

§10.7.B Native + Fleet Directives (D38-D55)

D40Governance parity by default — fleet inherits Asawa governance behavior
D41Native Phase 3 cohort pivot — validate on new external clients first
D42Visibility before influence — H↔Sutra v1.0 instruments before optimizing

§10.8 Cross-cutting threads

Citations added 2026-05-13 per R12 DeepSeek P2 — each thread now traces to its source documents.

  • Fractal-shape across scales — Unit structure repeats at holding → portfolio → project → task level; governance envelope nests identically at every depth. Sources: holding/research/2026-04-22-unit-foundation-AE-grid.md · holding/research/2026-04-24-core-unit-science-synthesis.md · ADR-016 replica-as-isolated-user-kit.
  • Input-driven, not output-driven — Cynefin gate · Architecture-as-Discipline · codification ladder demand situation analysis before pattern selection. Sources: holding/research/2026-04-29-architecture-method.md · holding/research/2026-04-24-core-unit-science-synthesis.md (Cynefin gate).
  • Codify → enforce → promote — founder meta-pattern (notice → extract → mandatory → instrument → propagate) appears in every sub-section from primitives to permissions. Sources: holding/research/2026-04-22-unit-foundation-AE-grid.md · D6 · D55.
  • Plugin-first default — every capability, including Native itself, starts at L0 before earning L1 or L2 (PROTO-021 + D38). Sources: holding/research/2026-04-23-build-layer-protocol-design.md · D38.
  • Questions before patterns — 6-discipline method + 12 forced questions reject solutionism; every finding starts with a Problem Statement (PS-1 through PS-22). Sources: holding/research/2026-04-29-architecture-method.md · holding/research/_archive/native-v1.x/.../2026-04-29-native-v1.0-final-architecture.md.
  • Provenance over authority — ADRs · codex rounds · founder directions all carry explicit provenance; decisions never opaque. Sources: ADR-007 decision-provenance-schema · ADR-009 approval-gate-primitive · holding/FOUNDER-DIRECTIONS.md (append-only audit trail).
  • ≤1700 LoC discipline — net-new glue constraint as forcing function for simplicity; threads through stack · skills · connector decisions. Source: holding/research/_archive/native-v1.x/.../2026-04-29-native-v1.0-final-architecture.md (budget constraint section).

Rationale: Founder ask 2026-05-13 — "make a list of all the things which we have implemented...check whether the document contains those or not." L3 drill-down — S1 Intent (B1) · 4 viewpoints [WORKED SAMPLE]

One L2/L3 block shown from all 4 viewpoints. Every other subsystem reuses this exact shape. (Standalone copy: tech-design/HLD/subsystem-b1-intent.html.)

Viewpoint 1 — User (what the operator experiences):

 operator: "remind me to check the deploy at 5pm"
     |
     v
 [ plain words in -- no command, no schema, no form ]
     |
     v
 Native: "Got it -- TASK, routing to Action. Confidence 0.81.
          Also ping you on Slack at 5pm? (y/n)"
     |
     v
 [ if unclear (conf < 0.6) -> ONE clarifying question; never guess ]

Viewpoint 2 — System (runtime mechanics):

 raw_text --> [Intake Capture] --> .tmp/native-utterance.txt
                   |
                   v
             [Classifier] (single LLM call)
                   |
                   v
      { intent, confidence, routed_to, clarify }
                   +--> intake_log.jsonl (fsync, append-only, ADR-013)
                   |
           conf >= 0.6 ? --no--> [Clarify loop] -> operator
                   | yes
                   v
      EngineEvent: intake.classified --> routed_to (B2/B3...)

Viewpoint 3 — AI (agentic layer):

 prompt = system(role + HOW dims) + utterance + trusted-names
     |
     v
 [ LLM classify ] -> intent in {ask,task,direction,feedback}
     |               + sub-intents + confidence
     v
 HOW dimension selected: "decompose" vs "direct" vs "consult"
     |
     v
 EVAL GATE: golden set (50) >= 90% correct-route  <- agentic accept bar
     |
     v
 low-conf -> clarify  [test-before-conclude: never assert a guess]

Viewpoint 4 — Everything (stitched + connections):

 +------------------- B1 INTENT (integrated) ----------------------+
 | USER:    plain words in     ->  confirm / 1 clarify out         |
 | SYSTEM:  capture -> classify -> conf gate -> log -> route       |
 | AI:      LLM intent + HOW-dim -> eval gate -> no-fabricate      |
 +----------------------------------------------------------------+
      |             |              |               |
      v             v              v               v
  runs inside   routes to      gated by        every turn
  PLATFORM      B2/B3 by       B5 GOVERN       logged to
  (daemon)      intent_type    (authority+HS)  OBSERVABILITY
L3 drill-down — remaining subsystems [SCAFFOLD — pending shape approval]

Each gets the same 4-viewpoint shape as B1 above, once the cascade is blessed:

  1. 6 Surfaces — ROUTE · RUN · GATE · EMERGE · AUDIT · TENANT (the engine spine — highest priority)
  2. CoS pillars — B2 Context · B3 Action · B4 Comms · B5 Governance (B1 done above)
  3. Native Platform — daemon / registry+effector / tenant isolation
  4. Connector — transport (Slack · email · web · A2A v2)
  5. Observability — 3-channel audit + metrics + Daily Pulse
  6. Distribution — T1→T4 tier rollout

Decision pending — confirm subsystem axis (surfaces-primary vs pillars-primary vs both) + ASCII vs SVG/Mermaid, then these generate in one pass.

§11.2 LLD — component design

Low-Level Design for component implementers. 5 pillar mechanisms + 4 canon catalogs.

LLD/B1 — Intent Layer

Operator voice: "Let me ask in my own words" (Pillar B1).

Mechanism scope: How freeform operator utterance compiles into typed intent + sub-intents. Show-by-example + trusted-names shortcut.

Canon: sutra/os/native/blocks/ (B1 spec) · sutra/os/native/pillars/ · PRD §2.B B1 · ADR-012.

Planned sections:

  1. Input — operator utterance shape + supported variations
  2. Parser — freeform → typed intent (intent_type · sub-intents · pre-declared schema)
  3. Show-by-example mechanism — operator demonstrates once, Native re-runs
  4. Trusted-names shortcut — disambiguation via operator-set aliases
  5. Refinement loop — operator narrows; not a fresh ASK
  6. Output — intake artifact written to B5.a (active session)
  7. Acceptance criteria · failure modes · test strategy

LLD/B2 — Context Retrieval

Operator voice: "Bring me the world's best thinking, plus my own" (Pillar B2).

Mechanism scope: 3-source assembly (my own · world's best · peer Sutras v2+). Ranking algo (metadata v1 / semantic v2 via F1) + budget allocator.

Canon: sutra/os/native/blocks/ (B2 spec) · PRD §2.B B2 · PRD §1.4.5 F1 gates.

Planned sections:

  1. 3-source assembly — B2.a my own · B2.b world's best · B2.c peer Sutras (v2+)
  2. B2.a retrieval — from B5 system-of-record (Charter scope · recency · operator tags)
  3. B2.b retrieval — research-on-the-fly + curated knowledge
  4. Ranking algorithm — v1 metadata-driven · v2 semantic via F1
  5. Budget allocator — token budget per source + total
  6. "Show me the context used" surface — ranked artifact list
  7. B2.c v2+ federation protocol (deferred)

LLD/B3 — Action Engine

Operator voice: "Think with me where I must; do the rest yourself, end-to-end" (Pillar B3).

Mechanism scope: Method routing + escalation engine (smart auto vs ask). THINK/DO/PRESENT mode decomposition.

Canon: sutra/os/native/blocks/ (B3 spec) · PRD §2.B B3 · sutra/os/engines/ADAPTIVE-PROTOCOL.md.

Planned sections:

  1. Method routing — Task Framework phases · per-tier method (T0-T3)
  2. B3a THINK — brainstorm partnership mode
  3. B3b DO — auto-execute end-to-end (path-known)
  4. B3c PRESENT — decision-ready output rendering
  5. Smart escalation — when ask vs auto (6-dim Authority gate)
  6. Iterative refinement (operator narrows without fresh ASK)
  7. Failure modes · retry · escalation cascade

LLD/B4 — Connector Comms

Operator voice: "Talk to my people and the world for me" (Pillar B4).

Mechanism scope: Connector transport semantics. Audience-calibrated voice. v1 log-only A2A stub; v2+ full A2A.

Canon: sutra/os/native/blocks/ (B4 spec) · PRD §2.B B4 · sutra/marketplace/plugin/connectors/.

Planned sections:

  1. Connector transport — Slack · email · web (v1) · phone · browser (v2+)
  2. Audience-calibrated voice — board / founder / team / client / vendor
  3. Outbound message construction — pillar B3 → audience tone → connector send
  4. Inbound routing — connector → B3 next-action
  5. Authority gate per stakeholder · tool · dollar
  6. v1 A2A log-only stub; v2+ full A2A protocol
  7. Failure modes — connector down · auth fail · message format mismatch

LLD/B5 — Governance Substrate

Operator voice: "Remember, explain, and keep it mine" (Pillar B5).

Mechanism scope: Multi-tenant state machine + DecisionProvenance audit + replay + learned patterns (k=4).

Canon: sutra/os/native/blocks/ (B5 spec) · PRD §2.B B5 · ADR-007 · ADR-006.

Planned sections:

  1. 4-horizon state model — B5.a active · B5.b portfolio · B5.c audit · B5.d learned
  2. B5.a active session — minutes/hours, current-in-flight state
  3. B5.b portfolio in-flight — multi-task state machine (hard problem deferred)
  4. B5.c audit + replay — DecisionProvenance + replay-window (≤30s for single-day)
  5. B5.d learned patterns — emergence threshold k=4
  6. Cross-tenant boundary enforcement (HS-3)
  7. Standing prefs · persistence · recovery

LLD/Primitives — 10 canon types

Indexed view into canon primitives. Each primitive has its own deep spec under canon.

Canon: sutra/os/native/primitives/ (canon authority).

  1. Domain (operator-visible accountability)
  2. Charter (fixed/flexible scope contract)
  3. Workflow (typed step graph)
  4. Step (typed config · reuse_tag · return_contract)
  5. Trigger (event-driven workflow start)
  6. ExecutionResult (state machine output)
  7. EngineEvent (26 typed events)
  8. Tenant (Human-Org boundary)
  9. Approval (Authority gate primitive)
  10. DecisionProvenance (3-channel audit row)

Operator-visible failure behavior baseline shared across all Layer-B products. Engineering implements as runtime guards.

  1. HS-1 — Cross-tenant access deny
  2. HS-2 — Schema violation reject
  3. HS-3 — Tenant boundary default-deny
  4. HS-4 — Silent audit fail-open detection
  5. HS-5..HS-8 (TBD — names pending)
  6. Operator-facing surface when fired
  7. Override paths (Authority gate)

LLD/Surfaces — 6 orchestration surfaces

ROUTE · RUN · GATE · EMERGE · AUDIT · TENANT — each owns part of the workforce deployment lifecycle. Each surface is a contract, not a UI.

Canon: sutra/os/native/surfaces/ · ADR-004 registry-effector · ADR-010 organic-emergence.

  1. ROUTE — intake routing surface
  2. RUN — workflow execution surface
  3. GATE — Authority Model + approval surface
  4. EMERGE — pattern detection + learned automation surface
  5. AUDIT — DecisionProvenance + replay surface
  6. TENANT — multi-tenancy boundary surface
  7. Surface↔surface interaction map
  8. Per-surface engine event emission rules

Wire-level reference for every interface. For HLD context see HLD/04 Interfaces above.

§12 ADR Catalog — Decisions (WHY) #

Architectural Decision Records — rationale behind every architectural choice. ADR-018 carries the SHARED invariant across all Layer-B products; per-product specialization in ADR-NNN-<product>-pattern.md per ADR-020.

Status: 19 ADRs ratified (001-019). ADR-020 PROPOSED 2026-05-13 — codex r19 verdict CHANGES-REQUIRED with 5 findings (1 P1 + 4 P2). Findings at .enforcement/codex-reviews/2026-05-20-adr-020-r19-codex.log.

§12.1 ADR-001..020 table

ADRTitleStatusOne-line summary
001h-sutra-9cell-gridratified9-cell × 3-tag classification schema for every founder input
002out-direct-3checkratifiedOutput trace + direct execution + 3-check governance pattern
003permissions-mcp-and-first-time-editratifiedPermission model for MCP servers + first-time edit gate
004registry-and-effector-splitratifiedRegistry (audit) vs effector (mutator) separation in Native
005host-llm-host-selectionratifiedPer-workflow host LLM selection (claude · codex · deepseek)
006tenant-isolation-domain-fieldratifiedTenant isolation enforced via Domain field on every primitive
007decision-provenance-schemaratified3-channel append-only audit row per decision
008markdown-vs-code-authoritative-stateratifiedMarkdown is doctrine; code is runtime; which wins when they disagree
009approval-gate-primitiveratifiedApproval as a first-class primitive (not a hook)
010organic-emergence-propose-approveratifiedPattern detection proposes; operator approves; never silent automation
011on-failure-policy-five-setratified5-set failure policy enumeration (retry · escalate · skip · ask · halt)
012pnc-typed-parser-over-proseratifiedPre-call schema + post-call validation over prose-only LLM contract
013telemetry-sink-fsync-jsonlratifiedJSONL + fsync as telemetry sink (no silent loss)
014depth-router-via-workflowratifiedDepth (token budget) routed via workflow primitive
015standalone-h-sutra-in-nativeratifiedH-Sutra layer as standalone primitive within Native
016replica-as-isolated-user-kitratifiedAsawa replica isolated under user-kit for dogfood
017cron-daemon-cadence-tickratifiedCron-style daemon cadence for periodic surfaces
018agentic-systems-patternratifiedOperator-in-loop on consequential decisions; deterministic guardrails around stochastic core (SHARED across all Layer-B products)
019design-product-tech-bridgeratifiedBridge contract — design ↔ product ↔ tech traceability table
020layer-b-product-authoring-templatePROPOSED 2026-05-13Section set + canon additions every new Layer-B Product PRD must include (codex r19 CHANGES-REQUIRED)

R6 audit: 5 core promotions landed. R7 hierarchy: 3 sections landed. Phase 2 design detail forthcoming at design-v2-deep-doc.html. Tech-parts (domain models · hooks · agentic-systems-connection) queued for next session via R8 consult. Single source of truth for the entire Project Native system v1.
